563   DEBAT DE POLITIQUE GENERALE (suite)  GENERAL POLICY DEBATE (continued)  DEBATE DE POLITICA GENERAL (continuaci6n)  OBCYXIIEHHE BOITPOCOB OBILEH [TOJIMTHUKH (npoyonxenne)  (gli) Talat) Tools} 2 le  BEERS C5 )
1. The PRESIDENT:  Ladies and gentlemen, I declare open the fifteenth plenary meeting. Let me recall that we have a very  long list of speakers that we are trying to finish today; may I reiterate my hope that speakers will remain within  the time-limits. I now give the floor to the first speaker this afternoon, the Hon. Dr William F. Shija, Minister  for Science, Technology and Higher Education and head of the delegation of the United Republic of Tanzania.
2.1 Mr SHIJA (United Republic of Tanzania) (Statement delivered in Kiswahili; English translation  provided by the delegation):  Mr President, Mr Chairman of the Executive Board, Mr Director-General, Honourable Ministers and  heads of delegation, Your Excellencies, distinguished delegates, ladies and gentlemen, on behalf of the  Government of the United Republic of Tanzania, and in my own name, I take this opportunity to congratulate  you on your deserved election to preside over the proceedings and deliberations of this twenty-sixth session of  the UNESCO General Conference. We in Tanzania cherish your contribution to the work on the History of  Africa. We believe that such works will be very useful to future African generations and to the world at large.
2.2 May I also take this opportunity to thank the Government and people of France for the warm welcome  and hospitality extended to my delegation. It is this hospitality by the French people that will enable Member  States to deal effectively with all the items on the agenda related to peace, unity and development in UNESCO's  fields of competence. I would like, therefore, to record my delegation's deep appreciation of the message  contained in the address by H.E. President François Mitterrand. May 1 also take this opportunity to congratulate  the new Member States who have joined UNESCO.
2.3 Mr President, I will now turn to the important actions and activities accomplished by UNESCO during  the 1990-1991 biennium, particularly those in which Tanzania participated.
24 The recommendations of the Jomtien World Conference on Education for All, as stated in the  framework of action, have given added impetus to our continuing efforts to provide education for all our people.  As a follow-up, Tanzania organized a national conference on education and culture through which our  commitment to universal primary education (UPE), functional literacy, the rural press, vocational training and  other related schemes was reiterated and new strategies and programmes were adopted. For example, even  though we have attained a literacy rate of 93 per cent, we believe there is need to renew our educational system,  especially at primary level. We need more science equipment and laboratories for schools, and to expand the  availability of teaching aids and books, particularly for science teaching.
2.5 To this end I wish, on behalf of my government, to thank UNESCO, other United Nations agencies,  non-governmental organizations and individual governments, who have assisted us in these areas. However, in  view of our weak resource base, Tanzania hopes that the organizations and individual Member States will  continue to support its basic education sector in the problem areas outlined above. Once these shortcomings are  put right young Tanzanians will be able to enter the twenty-first century with more hope and confidence.
2.6 Furthermore, the distance education seminar held in Tanzania last year under the Priority: Africa’  programme, was timely for the country because it served as an important spring-board for attaining the  objectives of education for all. In pursuit of this programme, Tanzania will use the distance-teaching approach  as an educational strategy to make education available to as many people as possible, even at the tertiary level.  There are plans, for example, to establish an open university next year which will allow many men and women  to pursue university education through distance teaching. My government appeals to UNESCO and Member  States with the required experience and resources to collaborate with us in this programme since co-operation  always makes it possible for such ventures to take off with minimum hindrance. Our decision to set up an open  university neither compromises nor erodes our well-founded moral commitment to provide universal primary  education and functional literacy (including the rural press), which we have been pursuing for many years now.  The planned institution will in fact complement these other efforts.
2.7 Mr President, our delegation commends the Director-General's programme for implementing the  World Decade for Cultural Development, particularly those activities designed to underline the link between  culture and development. We believe that preventive and curative maintenance of structures, infrastructures,  machinery and equipment is a prerequisite for sustainable development in the developing world. We would  like, therefore, to inform the Director-General that Tanzania offers to play host to the proposed regional  workshop on a culture of maintenance for Africa. At the same time, we would. like to reiterate our support of  the Iron Route project proposed by the Republic of Mozambique.
2.8 Mr President, may I now turn to Major Programme Area [V (Communication in the service of  humanity). While endorsing the general objectives and planned programmes of action, Tanzania firmly  believes that more affirmative action should be taken to help developing nations to build capacities at two  levels: within national borders and across national borders. Only when this is done will it be possible for the  entire world to speak about and practise a wider and better balanced dissemination of information without any  obstacle to the freedom of expression. Reduction of the disparities in communication resources and technologics  that now divide the North from the South is a prerequisite for the democratization of information and  communication processes. The impact of communication technologies on the cultures of developing countries  must also be studied with a view to helping these countries deal with the negative aspects of cultural influence.
2.9 Mr President, it is very disturbing that while part of humanity 1s today experiencing unprecedented  achievements in science and technology, the majority of developing countries are stagnating and continuing to  be consumers of research products, including agricultural products, from the developed North. Yet all of us are  part of and subscribe to the crucial importance of science and technology and their application to the  development of all Member States, collectively and individually. Consequently, we are of the view that it would  be opportune now for UNESCO and its Member States to start thinking about a world decade for scientific and  technological development, whose objectives would be similar to those of the World Decade for Cultural  Development. For its part, and on the basis of this conviction, Tanzania has created the Ministry of Science,  Technology and Higher Education to spearhead the use of science and technology as part of its strategy to solve  immediate and future development problems.
2.10 Mr President, my government believes that no economic development and no environmental protection  can take place without the input of well-trained engineers and technicians. This 1s why Tanzania has submitted  a second draft resolution urging UNESCO to find ways to improve manpower development in the engineering  and technical fields for Africa. It is with this mission in mind that we strongly recommend UNESCO to  promote training in the management of science and technology in developing Member States, particularly in  the area of university/industry co-operation as conceived by the engineering and technology division of the  Natural Sciences Sector of UNESCO. This is work in which Tanzania would very much like to participate.
2.11 My second point, Mr President, is to share my views, and those of my delegation, with my colleagues  concerning the role of UNESCO in the unfolding political map. No doubt a new political map of the world 1s  taking shape. The end of the Cold War, the resulting rebirth of States in Eastern Europe, the independence of  Namibia and the emerging trends in South Africa towards racial harmony and the dismantling of apartheid, the  current attempts to end hostilities in the Middle East and to find a peaceful solution to the Palestinian  question - all these are issues to be considered by the designers of the new political map of the world.
2.12 This situation poses a great challenge to all of us. It is not a time for complacency or even celebration;  indeed, Mr President, it is difficult to tell where the changing world is heading. While the old world order - the  order of ideological confrontation - was based on the balance of power, the new world order has not yet taken  shape especially in the absence of power balance. In such a situation, Mr President, the world community needs  the United Nations and its agencies more than ever before. UNESCO is irreplaceable.
2.13 This brings me to my contention that membership of UNESCO creates an obligation - a kind of socio-  political responsibility on the part of members of the world community who are concerned about a world of  peace, a world of co-operation, a world of dialogue and understanding between nations. Since UNESCO is  moving towards its fiftieth anniversary, has not the time come, Mr President, for it to undertake a study of  itself? the new world order with its new challenges may require a new UNESCO. That new UNESCO can only  be designed on the basis of a proper understanding of the performance of the old one. What I am proposing,  Mr President, is not an inventory of the numerous activitics undertaken by the Organization, but rather a study  of the impact of those activities on the world community.
2.14 Mr President, the world needs intellectual guidance. A number of global questions need objective  examination. The search for democracy in the new States, at a time when the forces which had laid claim to the  concept and practice of democracy in the old States have collapsed, is a matter that requires careful appraisal of  historical and existing socio-cultural settings. I do not think the question now is whether or not a country  chooses democracy, but rather what form of democracy is most suitable.
2.15 Mr President, Tanzania believes that the present and future world problems, and the challenge they  represent for UNESCO as an intellectual body, require UNESCO to continue to utilize local experts in  executing its programmes in various regions. In the context of developing countries, this would foster the  South-South co-operation advocated by, among others, Mwalimu Julius K. Nyerere and reiterated by  H.E. President Ali Hassan Mwinyi. We believe that this approach should also be part of UNESCO's  decentralization policy.
2.16 Mr President, in the light of current and future political, economic, social and cultural problems,  Tanzania takes note of and supports the reform proposed by the Government of Japan, and the proposal  submitted by Germany. However, Tanzania would like to ensure that certain basic features of this important  and valuable Organization are retained. The world of today and tomorrow has need of an intellectual and  practical forum for works of the mind. UNESCO should set an example of democracy, which is a basic  principle for the preservation of world peace and equality. It is this fundamental democratic requirement that  leads us to reiterate that the present link between the Executive Board and the General Conference should be  maintained.
2.17 Lastly, Mr President, my government supports the initiatives taken by the Executive Board to look into  its own working methods by setting up a group of experts in financial and administrative matters. I would urge  that in both cases the principle of equitable geographical distribution and the balanced representation of  cultural and regional groups be adhered to. I thank you.
3. The PRESIDENT:  I thank the head of the delegation of the United Republic of Tanzania. Our next speaker is  H.E. Mr Rene Steichen, Deputy Minister of Cultural Affairs and Scientific Research and head of the delegation  of Luxembourg.
4.1 M. STEICHEN (Luxembourg) :  Monsieur le Président, Monsieur le Directeur général, Mesdames et Messieurs, je voudrais me joindre  aux éminents orateurs qui m'ont précédé à cette tribune pour vous féliciter, Monsieur le Président, de votre  brillante élection à la présidence de cette Conférence. La délégation luxembourgeoise est convaincue que, sous  votre direction éclairée, la vingt-sixième session de la Conférence générale de l'UNESCO sera conduite à un  plein succès, grâce à vos grandes qualités diplomatiques et votre parfaite connaissance du fonctionnement de  l'UNESCO et des autres organisations onusiennes.
4,2 Monsieur le Directeur général, il me tient à coeur aussi de vous remercier personnellement, ainsi que  tous vos collaborateurs, de la qualité des documents qui nous ont été soumis pour discussion et décision. Je  voudrais vous assurer du soutien confiant et actif du gouvernement et de l'Etat luxembourgeois dans la  réalisation des objectifs et programmes que nous allons adopter à la présente session.
4.3 Monsieur le Président, Monsieur le Directeur général, Mesdames, Messieurs, après une période de  troubles et de crises, des signes d'espoir et de renouveau s'affirment au sein de l'Organisation. De concert avec -  ses partenaires de la Communauté européenne, le Luxembourg se félicite qu'un consensus ait pu etre trouvé  pour la réforme de l'UNESCO. La lutte contre l'analphabétisme est entrée dans sa phase décisive. La  coopération de l'UNESCO avec les autres institutions de l'ONU s'est renforcée. La Décennie mondiale du  développement culturel a souligné l'importance des valeurs immatérielles dans un monde menacé par la  croissance et la consommation à outrance. Enfin, l'UNESCO a largement retrouvé le rôle que lui impartit son  Acte constitutif et qui se concrétise dans le Projet de programme et de budget pour 1992-1993.
4.4 Je me réjouis tout particulièrement des premiers résultats tangibles obtenus dans la lutte contre  l'analphabétisme à la suite de la Conférence de Jomtien. Le combat contre l'analphabétisme n'est pas seulement  le combat contre l'ignorance. C'est en même temps un combat contre les préjugés, l'intolérance et le racisme,  contre le sous-développement et son cortège de fléaux, contre les inégalités de tout ordre, l'exploitation et le  totalitarisme. C'est enfin, et avant tout, un combat pour la dignité humaine. Aussi mon pays soutient-il tous les
566   efforts de l'UNESCO tendant à éradiquer l'analphabétisme dans les prochaines décennies. Si faibles que soient  nos moyens, nous avons voulu associer nos efforts au grand élan de solidarité suscité par l'Année internationale  de l'alphabétisation, notamment par un modeste projet de coopération au Sénégal réalisé sur l'initiative de notre  Commission nationale pour l'UNESCO.
4.5 Point de départ d'une campagne mondiale en faveur de l'éducation pour tous, la Conférence de Jomtien  a également renforcé la coopération entre l'UNESCO et les autres institutions des Nations Unies engagées dans  le même combat : le PNUD, l'UNICEF et la Banque mondiale. Cette nouvelle coopération pourrait encore  inclure d'autres organisations comme la FAO, le Comité intergouvernemental de la science et de la technique  au service du développement ainsi que l'Université des Nations Unies. Tout en évitant les doubles emplois, une  telle coopération renforce les possibilités d'action complémentaires de chaque institution onusienne. Au-delà de  ses résultats immédiats, Jomtien nous a donné une double leçon. C'est uniquement par la concertation, et non  dans un esprit de concurrence mesquin, qu'on peut espérer résoudre les grands problèmes de notre époque. C'est  dans l'action commune que chaque institution du système des Nations Unies retrouve sa place et peut s'affirmer  dans le domaine de sa compétence.
4.6 Si la Décennie mondiale du développement culturel n'a pas encore donné tous les résultats qu'on  pouvait espérer, elle a pour le moins souligné la primauté, pour l'avenir, de la dimension culturelle du  développement. Face aux multiples périls du monde moderne, il importe, plus que jamais, de sauvegarder les  identités culturelles, d'élargir la participation à la vie culturelle, de promouvoir la coopération internationale et  d'accorder une place centrale aux valeurs culturelles et humaines dans le développement économique et  technologique.
4,7 Monsieur le Président, Monsieur le Directeur général, Mesdames, Messieurs, la délégation de mon  pays constate avec satisfaction que, dans l'ensemble, le Projet de programme et de budget pour 1992-1993 est  conforme aux orientations générales du Plan à moyen terme pour 1990-1995. Elle retient tout particulièrement  les progrès accomplis en matière de concentration et de coopération intersectorielle dans l'ensemble du  programme. Elle approuve la volonté de renforcer les liens de coopération avec les organisations non  gouvernementales et d'accroître la participation des commissions nationales à la mise en oeuvre du programme.
4.8 Ce dernier point nous tient particulièrement à coeur. Sans l'apport des commissions nationales, l'action  de l'UNESCO serait coupée de ses racines locales et vitales. Dans cette optique, je ne voudrais pas passer sous  silence l'action de notre propre Commission qui, au cours des dernières années, a su prendre un certain nombre  d'initiatives heureuses malgré les moyens limités dont elle dispose. Outre les différentes manifestations  culturelles qu'elle organise, elle a, par exemple, réalisé une anthologie sur le thème de la paix, élaboré un  modèle type d'enseignement multiculturel, organisé un projet de coopération dans le cadre de l'Année  internationale de l'alphabétisation, renforcé l'image et la présence de l'UNESCO dans la presse écrite et parlée.  À cet égard, je voudrais vous indiquer qu'un sondage représentatif a révélé que l'UNESCO figure parmi les  organisations les plus connues au Luxembourg.
4.9 Monsieur le Président, Monsieur le Directeur général, Mesdames, Messieurs, si la situation actuelle de  l'UNESCO permet d'envisager son avenir avec sérénité, il reste, hélas, une ombre au tableau : l'Organisation  n'a toujours pas retrouvé toute son universalité, indissociable de son essence et de sa mission première. L'espoir  que notre Organisation - où nous saluons l'entrée des républiques baltes - retrouvera sous peu son caractère  pleinement universel s'appuie, entre autres, sur le fait indéniable que, dans l'incroyable accélération de l'histoire  que nous vivons depuis les changements intervenus en Europe depuis deux ans notamment, le climat  international s'est incontestablement amélioré. Les données politiques dans le monde se sont fondamentalement  transformées. La confrontation idéologique entre l'Est et l'Ouest a cédé le pas à la coopération. À cette  évolution heureuse, s'ajoute le fait que l'ONU a gagné une crédibilité nouvelle dont toute la famille onusienne  est appelée à bénéficier.
4.10 = Voilà qui appelle plus que jamais l'universalité et la cohésion de l'UNESCO. Cela est d'autant plus vrai  que la paix dans le monde risque de devenir l'otage d'un autre conflit : celui qui est latent entre les pays  hautement industrialisés et ceux qui ne le sont pas. Or, la délégation du Luxembourg est convaincue que  l'UNESCO, précisément, a une contribution essentielle à apporter pour aider à surmonter ce clivage  fondamental. Nous sommes, en effet, persuadés que ce sera grâce à l'éducation et à la culture que la coopération  internationale pourra le plus efficacement combler des fossés qu'on aurait tort de croire seulement économiques.
4.11 Cette situation, dont il ne nous appartient pas d'analyser les causes, nous paraît d'autant plus  déplorable que le monde actuel, plus que jamais, a besoin de l'UNESCO. La contribution de l'UNESCO est  indispensable pour résoudre quelques-uns des grands problèmes de notre époque : la drogue, la violence et la  criminalité, le danger des manipulations génétiques, les déviations de la recherche scientifique vers des fins  destructrices, la pollution inquiétante de l'air, du sol, et des mers, les multiples déséquilibres entre les pays  riches et les pays les plus démunis.
4.12 Sans la contribution essentielle de l'UNESCO, comment saurait-on concevoir un nouvel ordre mondial  fondé sur la paix et le respect des droits de l'homme ? Comment saurait-on vaincre l'ignorance, les préjugés,  l'intolérance et toutes les autres causes de conflit qui naissent "dans l'esprit des hommes” ? Quelle autre  organisation pourrait se substituer à ce forum intellectuel, scientifique et culturel qu'est l'UNESCO ? Seule une  organisation responsable au premier chef de l'état d'esprit de l'humanité peut la préparer à cette éthique  universelle dont on s'aperçoit de plus en plus qu'elle est indispensable à la conservation de la nature, à la  sauvegarde de la paix et, sans doute, à la survie de l'espèce humaine. Notre monde a besoin d'une conscience  universelle, notre monde a besoin de l'UNESCO. Je vous remercie de votre attention.
5. The PRESIDENT:  I thank the head of the delegation of Luxembourg. Our next speaker is the Hon. Nyimasata Sanneh-  Bojang, Deputy Minister of Education, Youth, Sports and Culture and head of the delegation of Gambia.
6.1 Ms SANNEH-BOJANG (Gambia):  Mr President, Mr Director-General, distinguished delegates, ladies and gentlemen, it is with the  greatest pleasure, Mr President, that I take this opportunity to join my distinguished colleagues in  congratulating you on your election as President of the twenty-sixth session of the General Conference of  UNESCO. You have already demonstrated that your wise guidance will lead our deliberations to a successful  conclusion. In the execution of your task I assure you of the full co-operation and support of my delegation.
6.2 At a time when profound and unprecedented changes are taking place in the world and when many  developing nations face a growing economic crisis, I wish to reaffirm our commitment to the fundamental  principles of UNESCO. We also reaffirm our support for the decision to focus attention during the period 1992-
1993 on the major challenges of peace, development and the protection of the environment.
6.3 As many of the old barriers which have threatened world peace for decades gradually disappear,  UNESCO is presented with new challenges and opportunities for promoting peace and understanding among  nations. I refer, Mr President, to ethnic differences which have resurfaced as nations try to rediscover their  national identities; to the increased problem of political refugees; to the widening gap between the rich and the  poor nations - exacerbated by the increasing debt burden; and to the continuing degradation of our fragile  environment. The last is of special concern to the least developed countries of the Sahelian region, which have  little potential for sustained industrial growth and where a hostile climate continues to impose great difficulties  in the provision of food and fuel for the growing population of the area. In the face of challenges of this  magnitude, we note with satisfaction the decision of the Organization to continue to reduce its range of  programmes in order to focus attention and achieve greater impact on chosen priority areas: education for all,  culture, science and the environment, and the transverse themes of women and youth. This initiative is  welcomed indeed, as it reflects, I believe, the priorities and current pressing concerns of developing countries.
6.4 We are also encouraged by the determination of UNESCO to continue with the process of streamlining  its operations to make them more cost-effective and responsive to the needs of Member States. In this context, -  we support the move towards reducing the number of conferences, and rationalizing documentation. We hope  that these reforms will release more resources for programme execution.
6.5 Mr President, the proposal to place greater emphasis on regional strategies within the framework of  the decentralization policy is commendable. We believe that this could facilitate greater regional co-operation  and the pooling of regional resources and experience towards the solution of regional problems. But to make  this move effective, and for it to achieve the desired objectives, it is essential that decentralization measures  should include the transfer to field units of responsibilities and adequate resources. If we can share regional  research activities; if we can share our practical experiences, and build up a regional corps of experts who can  bring to bear on our problems not only professional expertise but a profound understanding of local culture and  local political and economic circumstances; and if we can strengthen the operational capacity of National  Commissions, we shall be making a significant move towards regional and national self-reliance.
6.6 In this context, it is essential that consideration be given to providing support for National  Commissions in the least developed countries in the face of increasing pressures on limited budgetary  resources. This may require a review of the Charter of National Commissions for UNESCO, in particular  Article V, to facilitate an increased UNESCO contribution to the Commissions.
6.7 Mr President, let me now turn to the education component of UNESCO programmes. We are gratified  that our 15-year education policy, launched in 1988, was vindicated by the historic World Conference on  Education for All held in Jomtien in 1990. In view of this new policy we have embarked on a wide-ranging,  ambitious programme of educational reforms, the objectives of which are to increase access to formal basic  education from 60 per cent of the school-age population to 75 per cent, and to increase the transition rate from  primary school to middle school after six years from 35 per cent to 60 per cent in the first step towards  providing every Gambian child with a nine-year basic education cycle relevant to the practical needs of a  largely rural population. In a search for higher quality and a more cost-effective use of scarce resources, we  have started to strengthen our planning capacity, and reorganized the education sector management, introduced  new curricular and examination systems and expanded teacher-training programmes. We welcome, therefore,  UNESCO's support for training programmes for all categories of education personnel, especially for head  teachers who make a valuable contribution to the quality of our education system.
6.8 I wish to place on record that in this task we have received assistance not only from the World Bank,  UNESCO, UNICEF, and the African Development Bank but also from other international aid agencies, from  NGOs and from religious and charitable organizations. For this we are grateful; and we consequently welcome  UNESCO's decision to work towards maximizing co-operation and reducing duplication of effort by forging  closer links with aid agencies that have an interest in education.
6.9 As we endeavour to expand access to formal education and to upgrade the quality of our education, we  are conscious that in concentrating our limited resources on specific priorities within the formal education  system we are prevented by severe resource constraints from providing adequate support for other key areas of  educational development. Here, Mr President, I refer to non-formal education, technical and vocational  education, higher education, early childhood development and the education of children with special learning  needs. We are gratified to note that these areas feature prominently in the Draft Programme and Budget for
6.10 Non-formal education, including literacy and continuing education, has been under-resourced and  relies heavily on the support of non-governmental agencies. Literacy and adult education programmes which  emphasize the imparting of social and vocational skills to adults, particularly in rural areas, will enhance their  self-perception and their ability to play a meaningful role in national development.
6.11 The delivery of technical and vocational education must also be strengthened; conscious of the vital  importance of vocational training in human-resource development, we have embarked on a comprehensive  review of this sector aimed at preparing an action plan for the expansion of technical and vocational training  opportunities for school-leavers at all levels. We therefore welcome UNESCO's intervention in the area of  education and the world of work as described in Subprogramme 1.2.1, and wish to state now that we look  forward to collaborating with UNESCO for the improvement of our national capacity in this area.
6.12 The development of library services in the least developed countries is an area of need in which we  would urge greater UNESCO participation. Adults who achieve literacy and school-leavers eager for knowledge  need to have access to library services. The lack of such services could lead to a relapse into functional  illiteracy. There is a need for branch libraries situated in all major centres of population, stocked with a good  supply of educational and recreational materials, and for a reliable school library service capable of delivering  books on a regular basis to rural primary schools. We urge that this important subject be given priority in the  programmes for 1992-1993, We appreciate UNESCO's continued concern for improving the quality of higher  education and for increased regional co-operation at this level. We therefore invite the Organization to consider  the problems faced by small countries which have no universities or other higher educational institutions of  their own, and have no access to purpose-buiit regional higher institutions. The greater the success of our  education for all programme the greater will be the eventual demand for access to higher education. If we are  unable to provide appropriate facilities for higher education, we will be creating frustration among young  people and much human resource potential will be wasted. How, under such circumstances, do we provide the  opportunity for high-calibre secondary school-leavers to realize greater attainments and, more importantly, self-  fulfilment.
6.13 Mr President, I am pleased to mention a number of areas of major concern to UNESCO in which we  have already made some strides. The first is the effective involvement of women in the development process. In  the many years since their establishment our Women's Council and Women's Bureau have served as policy and  operational agencies respectively in support of women's affairs, and recently a World Bank assisted project on  women has been launched to provide a wide range of services and assistance for women in various Sectors.  Other initiatives related to women are featured in the government's programme for sustained development,  which provides for the increased involvement of women in education. We support those pilot programmes  aimed at strengthening the self-perception and confidence of women and at improving the access of women to  vocational education, and look forward to co-operating with UNESCO in their implementation.
6.14 The second area is the environment. We have established a national council on the environment and  have designed a national plan for the protection of the environment, through which environmental education is  to be integrated in the primary-school curriculum. In this context, also, we would wish to participate in  UNESCO's programmes for the promotion of public awareness of the relationship between environmental  preservation and the needs of development.
6.15 Mr President, aware of the significant role of culture in national development, we have launched a  national policy aimed at preserving, developing and promoting our cultural heritage. Within this policy  framework we have established a national council for arts and culture responsible for the promotion and  development of all aspects of our culture. We believe that these modest steps, taken at the national level, will  contribute significantly towards the objectives of the World Decade for Cultural Development. There remains a  great deal to be done in this area, particularly in the training of personnel in all the disciplines of culture, and  the preservation of monuments and historic sites.
6.16 Mr President, we are pleased to note also the emphasis given to the transverse theme of youth in all  major UNESCO programme areas, and to the need to integrate young people into international development.  The young people of today are faced with severe social problems such as unemployment and exposure to the  dangers of drugs and AIDS. It is essential that special attention be given to the search for practical solutions to  these problems. With this concern in mind we have formulated a national youth policy under which a national  youth bureau has been established to serve as a link between the government and young people, so that the  latter may discuss issues affecting them. Given the priority accorded to culture and youth in the Draft  Programme and Budget for 1992-1993, we hope to work closely with the Organization in the implementation  of our national policies in these sectors.
6.17 Finally, Mr President, with the ongoing reforms within UNESCO and the ambitious work programme  it has set itself for the next two years, we are convinced that our Organization is ready and equipped to confront  the challenges of these difficult times. What we accomplish together in the context of the Organization's  medium-term priorities will undoubtedly shape our vision of UNESCO for the coming century.
7. The PRESIDENT:  I thank the head of the delegation of Gambia. Our next speaker is H.E. Dr Manuel Faustino, Minister  of Education and head of the delegation of Cape Verde.
8.1 M. FAUSTINO (Cap-Vert) :  (Discours prononcé en portugais ; traduction française fournie par la délégation)  Monsieur le Président de la Conférence générale, Monsieur le Président du Conseil exécutif, Monsieur  le Directeur général de l'UNESCO, Messieurs les chefs des délégations, Messieurs les délégués, Mesdames et  Messieurs, au nom du gouvernement du Cap-Vert et de ma délégation, je félicite M. Bethwell Allan Ogot pour  son élection à la présidence de la Conférence générale. Notre conviction est que toute son expérience et la  connaissance qu'il a de notre Organisation seront mises au service de sa plus haute instance, la Conférence  générale. Je salue le Président provisoire de la Conférence générale, M. Anwar Ibrahim, qui a dirigé avec tant  de sagesse les travaux de la session précédente et je lui prédis les plus grands succès dans les nouvelles missions  qui sans aucun doute lui seront confiées, considérant les capacités que tous s'accordent à lui reconnaître.  J'aimerais également adresser un éloge au Président du Conseil exécutif, M. Yahya Aliyu, pour la façon  équilibrée et la compétence avec lesqueiles il dirige cet organe essentiel de contrôle de l'exécution du  programme de l'UNESCO. Je salue tout spécialement le Directeur général de l'UNESCO, M. Federico Mayor,  pour l'action hautement efficace qu'il a menée pendant les quatre années de son mandat qui sont déjà écoulées.  Un salut tout spécial aux nouveaux Etats membres de l'UNESCO : la Lituanie, l'Estonie, la Lettonie et Tuvalu.
570   La famille réunie autour de l'UNESCO s'élargit peu à peu, montrant au monde son universalité, ce qui est une  occasion de réjouissance pour toutes les nations qui ont embrassé les idéaux qu'elle défend.
8.2 Monsieur le Président, une des caractéristiques dominantes de notre époque est la tendance à la  démocratisation des relations internationales. Elle est la conséquence des processus de démocratisation à  l'oeuvre au sein d'Etats nationaux et en même temps, elle catalyse ces mêmes processus. Dans une dynamique  d'effets multiplicateurs mutuels, on assiste à une redéfinition de ces relations, à l'aube d'un temps nouveau, où  renaît l'espoir que l'utopie peut devenir possible.
8.3 Les infinies potentialités de la démocratisation politique exigent cependant, comme condition de sa  mise en oeuvre, qu'on surmonte l'obstacle de l'absence d'équité dans la répartition des biens et des services entre  les régions, les pays et les différentes couches sociales des nations.
8.4 En vérité, la fin de ce que l'on nommait la guerre froide, l'émergence vigoureuse de la société civile sur  la scène politique à divers niveaux et la prise de conscience de l'obsolescence des régimes d'exclusion politique  créent des conditions excellentes pour la résolution de problèmes qui n'en finissent pas d'affliger des pans  entiers de l'humanité. Cette tendance extrêmement positive facilite sans conteste cette résolution, mais elle  n'apporte pas elle-même les différentes solutions. Le tableau qui se dessine actuellement à l'échelle planétaire  permet aux hommes et aux nations de se retrouver face à face. Espérons qu'ils seront capables de se regarder,  qu'ils arriveront finalement à admettre que la misère et l'analphabétisme découlent dans une large mesure de la  répartition inégale des richesses et de ses conséquences. Nous arriverons sans aucun doute à admettre un jour  que la dégradation de l'environnement est surtout une question sociopolitique. Comme l'a affirmé le Président  Mitterrand : "On ne bâtit pas la démocratie du progrès sur la misère et l'injustice”.
8.5 Monsieur le Président, Mesdames, Messieurs, il est courant, lors des périodes comme celle que nous  vivons actuellement, que des aspirations plus ou moins légitimes se fassent jour avec une force incalculable.  Parfois, ces aspirations acquièrent une intensité irrépressible et finissent par compromettre leur propre  satisfaction. Cela arrive particulièrement lorsqu'elles ont été étouffées par la violence. Aujourd'hui surgissent un  peu partout des foyers de tension qui laissent présager de nouveaux arrangements. De tels mouvements  expriment fréquemment des revendications culturelles, nationales ou sociales légitimes. Cependant, elles ne  manquent pas de générer des tensions qui devront être surveillées. Simultanément, on observe des  comportements inquiétants d'intolérance envers les minorités, génératrices aussi d'instabilité. La complexité de  cette nouvelle situation impose que les relations entre peuples et Etats soient considérées de façon courageuse et  inventive.
8.6 C'est dans ce nouveau contexte que nous plaçons l'appel du Directeur général de l'UNESCO, évoquant  la nécessité d'un "contrat moral”. Cet appel lance un défi à la communauté internationale, réclame un nouvel  éclairage de la problématique du développement, de nouvelles responsabilités pour les acteurs  gouvernementaux et non gouvernementaux, exige fondamentalement la recherche de solutions aux problèmes  concrets. Dans ce contexte, l'UNESCO apparaît destinée à jouer un rôle de premier plan. D'ailleurs, cette  préoccupation va dans le sens de l'effort d'adaptation à ces temps nouveaux entrepris par l'UNESCO, qui  marque sa renaissance, comme l'a affirmé le Directeur général. Il est vrai qu'il s'agit là d'un processus de  reconversion totale qui devra permettre à l'Organisation d'assumer plemement sa vocation universelle et  d'intensifier la lutte contre les inégalités.
8.7 Monsieur le Président, comme nous l'avons affirmé tout à l'heure, la nouvelle conjoncture dans  laquelle nous vivons résulte de l'articulation de bouleversements qui se produisent dans différents espaces  nationaux et sur la scène internationale. Mon pays, le Cap-Vert, n'a pas manqué d'être touché par les grands  changements qui sont en train de modifier profondément la face politique du monde. Au début de cette année,  les structures politiques du Cap-Vert ont commencé d'être profondément transformées. Pour la première fois  depuis son accession à l'indépendance, 1l y à seize ans, le Cap-Vert a procédé à des élections libres dans un  cadre de démocratie pluripartite. La transition politique a eu lieu de façon absolument pacifique et aujourd'hui  le peuple cap-verdien s'efforce d'édifier un régime véritablement démocratique et d'extirper les maux de  l'exclusion politique. Comme de nombreux autres pays, nos moyens matériels sont extrêmement limités. Nous  sommes conscients des grandes difficultés que ces limitations nous imposent s'agissant du renforcement de la  démocratie. Mais nous savons également que le renforcement et la démocratisation des organisations  internationales seront d'une grande valeur pour les pays qui connaissent des conditions semblables aux nôtres.  C'est là l'une des principales raisons pour lesquelles nous accordons à l'UNESCO une importance toute  particulière et c'est dans cette perspective que nous nous situons puur examiner les questions en discussion.
8.8 Nous sommes un pays sahélien, avec une population majoritairement jeune, qui connait de sérieux  problèmes sur le plan de la condition féminine et qui fait ses premiers pas sur la voie de la démocratie  pluraliste. Pour cette raison, nous accordons une attention spéciale aux questions de l'environnement, de la  jeunesse, des femmes, et de la communication et l'information, Nous nous réjouissons donc de l'importance  attribuée à ces programmes transversaux.
8.9 Les changements souhaités déjà depuis un certain temps, au sein de l'UNESCO, trouvent là, dans la  nouvelle conjoncture internationale, un terrain favorable pour leur mise en oeuvre. Les réformes préconisées ne  font que refléter l'effort d'adaptation aux grands défis lancés à l'humanité dans le temps présent.
8.10 La réussite de l'UNESCO dépendra, par conséquent, en grande partie, du succès des réformes en cours,  un succès qui pourra se concrétiser uniquement grâce aux efforts et au soutien de tous. Comme le disait le  Directeur général, dans son discours d'ouverture au débat de politique générale, la réflexion devra être  dorénavant le phare de l'action et son meilleur pilote. Aussi toutes les propositions visant à améliorer les  structures et le fonctionnement de l'UNESCO seront les bienvenues, une fois dûment pondérés les avantages et  les inconvénients, sauvergardés les intérêts de tous les membres et respectés les droits de chaque Etat membre,  particulièrement les moins puissants. Une importante contribution vient d'être apportée par le Japon, pour cet  effort d'amélioration du rôle de l'UNESCO : le bon accueil fait à la proposition du Japon est hautement  significatif. Nous prenons note aussi des initiatives émanant des autres pays dans le sillage de la proposition de  ce pays asiatique, initiatives qui ont trouvé une expression particulière dans le projet de résolution de  l'Allemagne.
8.11 Monsieur le Président, Messieurs les chefs de délégation, Mesdames, Messieurs, l'UNESCO vient  d'achever la première phase du Plan à moyen terme avec un bilan positif, ce qui est la conséquence naturelle de  l'effort d'harmonisation avec la réalité. Les exemples que nous pourrions présenter de cet effort sont multiples ;  il serait donc fastidieux de les énumérer. Nous en citerons seulement trois, qui sont parlants : le programme  "Priorité Afrique", opportunément conçu sous l'orientation de M. le Directeur général ; l'attention croissante  qui continue d'être attribuée aux pays d'Europe centrale et d'Europe de l'Est ; enfin, très récemment, la création  d'une unité qui s'occupe exclusivement des pays les moins avancés (PMA).
8.12 — Un autre point digne d'être mentionné se rapporte aux réformes mises en oeuvre au sein du Secrétariat  de l'Organisation. En sont la preuve les mesures relatives à la gestion du personnel et aux regroupements  effectués. I! convient en outre de souligner le recrutement de coordonnateurs dans des domaines d'une haute  importance : l'environnement et l'enseignement de base.
8.13 La vingt-sixième session de la Conférence générale se trouve engagée dans l'analyse de documents  fondamentaux relatifs au Programme et budget de l'UNESCO pour le prochain exercice biennal. Les objectifs et  les stratégies définis dans la première phase du Plan à moyen terme sont toujours en vigueur. C'est la  concentration et l'économie visant une plus grande efficacité.
8.14 Monsieur le Président, avec votre permission, je porterai quelques appréciations générales sur les  principaux thèmes du Programme proposé pour le prochain exercice.
8.15 II n'est jamais superflu de mentionner la perspective globalisante du Programme et l'émergence de  quelques innovations. En ce qui nous touche de plus près, nous soulignerons : le lien étroit entre la lutte contre  l'analphabétisme et l'éducation des jeunes et des femmes ; l'importance attribuée à juste titre à l'enseignement  supérieur et à l'enseignement technique ; la coopération universitaire entre les pays en voie de développement ;  la formation de professeurs à tous les niveaux ; l'introduction de stratégies régionales ; les programmes  transversaux ; les projets mobilisateurs.
8.16 Nous insistons sur le fait que les actions préconisées en ce qui concerne l'environnement, la jeunesse,  la femme, la communication, la culture et l'informatique trouvent un écho dans l'ensemble des préoccupations  de tous les pays, particulièrement chez ceux qui sont dépourvus de ressources et sujets aux changements  provoqués par des écosystèmes fragiles. Nous pensons que le lien entre l'environnement, le développement et la  culture devra être encouragé et se traduire en projets concrets dans les pays appartenant au groupe des pays les  moins avancés. Une participation active de l'UNESCO à la préparation de la Conférence des Nations Unies sur  l'environnement et le développement est une forme concrète de la prise en compte de cette perspective,  particulièrement si, dans ce cadre, on attribue à la dimension culturelle du développement l'importance qui lui  est due. ;
8.17 Dans cet ordre de préoccupations, nous poursuivrons, avec l'UNESCO, tous nos efforts pour que la  Cidade Velha, au Cap-Vert, première cité fondée par les Portugais en terre africaine, soit inscrite sur la Liste du  patrimoine mondial.
8.18 Comme nous l'avons mentionné précédemment, des signes préoccupants d'intolérance envers des  minorités différentes s'obstinent à obscurcir l'horizon. En sa qualité de pays qui rejette catégoriquement tous les  types de discrimination, le Cap-Vert appuie toutes les actions de lutte contre le racisme et l'intolérance de toute  nature.
8.19 Un des instruments fondamentaux pour la réalisation des objectifs proposés est le budget de  l'UNESCO. À ce sujet, nous devons dire que nous donnons notre accord de principe à la philosophie adoptée  pour son élaboration. Cependant, nous ne pouvons manquer de manifester notre préoccupation face à la  diminution des ressources destinées à des activités déterminées ; aussi, recommandons-nous vivement  l'adoption de mesures énergiques visant à obtenir des ressources extrabudgétaires. Nous croyons que la  coopération triangulaire pourra être une bonne voie.
8.20 — Monsieur le Président, l'espace de la langue portugaise, bien plus qu'une simple zone linguistique ou  un ensemble de régions, englobe une réalité culturelle à multiples facettes que l'histoire continue de sculpter de  façon très riche. L'adoption du portugais comme langue de travail sera sans aucun doute la reconnaissance par  l'UNESCO de cette réalité et contribuera au renforcement de quelque chose qui s'est révélé depuis longtemps  irréversible et se nourrit d'initiatives concrètes. Citons à titre d'exemple le projet IRI/PALOP d'enseignement du  portugais et des mathématiques dans les pays africains de langue officielle portugaise, qui a obtenu le  parrainage de l'UNESCO, et la récente admission de l'Union des villes capitales de langue portugaise comme  ONG de catégorie C. Nous ne doutons pas que le développement naturel de la dynamique en cours sera la  création d'un Bureau de l'UNESCO dans les pays africains de langue officielle portugaise.
8.21 Les défis auxquels l'UNESCO doit aujourd'hui faire face sont importants et complexes et, bien que tous  les problèmes ne puissent être réglés dans le cadre de notre Organisation, il existe actuellement des conditions  favorables à leur solution. Pour cela, nos derniers mots seront des paroles d'espoir dans la capacité de  l'UNESCO de participer activement à la préparation du nouveau dialogue que l'homme paraît vouloir  inaugurer, Merci, Monsieur le Président.
9. The PRESIDENT:  I thank the head of the delegation of Cape Verde. Our next speaker is Mr Paula Bloomfield, Director  of Education and head of the delegation of Tonga.
10.1 Mr BLOOMFIELD (Tonga):  Mr President, Mr Director-General, Your Excellencies, ladies and gentlemen, I bring you most sincere  greetings from the Kingdom of Tonga. I would also like to take this opportunity to congratulate you,  Mr President, on your election to the most highly honoured task of steering the ship of our twenty-sixth General  Conference. Your wisdom and wealth of experience will, no doubt, serve as the foundation for the success of  this most important world forum. I have very fond memories, Mr President, of your most beautiful country  when you hosted the education ministers’ meeting in 1987.
102 — Tonga would also like to join the rest of the Member States of the UNESCO family in extending a  sincere and warm welcome to the new Member States: Estonia, Latvia, Lithuania, and our Pacific neighbour,  Tuvalu.
10.3 MrPresident, at the time of the 1989 General Conference we witnessed the disintegration of the Berlin  Wall, and saw East and West Germany reunited. At the 1991 General Conference, Estonia, Latvia and  Lithuania have become Member States. I wonder what 1993 holds for us. No doubt we shall have - let us pray -  welcome surprises.
10.4 The joining of Tuvalu, Mr President, has now increased the number of UNESCO Member States in the  Pacific to nine, and there is no doubt in my mind that there will be many more Pacific countries joining in the  very near future: hopefully, the Solomon Islands and Vanuatu, among others, will join. This is factual  testimony to the growing importance of UNESCO as a universal organization whose mission encompasses the  whole of mankind, as well as to the increasing awareness and desire of the nations of the Pacific for shared and  co-operative development in education, science, culture and communication. In saying this, Mr President, I
15   would like to register an appeal to this session of the General Conference, on behalf of the small Member States  of the Pacific, for more sympathetic recognition of their subregion in the major activities of the Organization  during the coming biennium, and particularly in the fields of science and culture.
10.5 Mr President, Tonga would also like to take this opportunity to thank and to congratulate the Director-  General for his leadership and the excellent work that has been evidenced in the preparation of the Draft  Programme and Budget for 1992-1993. Tonga fully supports and therefore commends the proposals for  adoption.
10.6 Mr President, thanks to the excellent leadership of the Director-General, the effective support of the  Assistant Directors-General, and the tireless efforts and commitment of UNESCO Headquarters in Paris, the  Director and staff of the Regional Office for Asia and the Pacific, in Bangkok, and the Office for the Pacific  States in Apia, a number of projects have been successfully implemented in the Pacific subregion since the last  General Conference in 1989. I would like to emphasize that most of the Pacific countries have effectively  participated and greatly benefited from these excellent projects.
10.7 In the area of education, much work has been carried out to prepare current pipeline projects. These  include the Pacific educational management system (EMIS); the Pacific educational assessment project; the  primary teachers in-service support network; the Pacific primary and literacy programme; the project on  vocational education in Pacific secondary schools; and the Pacific educational publishing project.
10.8 In the area of communication, the Pacific journalism training and development of the printed media  project (PACJOURN) and the Pacific video training project (PACVIDEO) have been successfully launched and  effectively implemented for the countries of the region. The countries concerned are very thankful and I would  like to register our sincere gratitude to UNESCO for the assistance provided.
10.9 You may have noticed, Mr President, that I have not mentioned anything in the areas of science and  culture. This is not to say that UNESCO has not been attending to the Pacific in these vital areas of its  mandate. Some efforts have been made and progress achieved, of course. The recent establishment of the  science post in the UNESCO Subregional Office in Apia is particularly welcome and, we believe, marks the  beginning of a UNESCO commitment to the development of science and especially marine sctence and  technology in the Pacific. This is only logical, since the sea is both home and life for the people of the Pacific.  Yet a study of document 26 C/5 will reveal that, unlike education with its 1/4 million dollars earmarked for the  Pacific, a mere $8,000 has been foreseen thus far for the subregion in the field of science.
10.10 As for the promotion of culture, I would like to register the concern of the Pacific Member States at the  withdrawal of the culture post from the Apia Office and, on behalf of those countries, I would like to beg the  Director-General to reconsider this issue. Since reinstatement of the cultural adviser's post has not been  foreseen in the draft budget for the coming biennium, we would recommend that the strategy of redeployment  and redesignation might satisfy both the Executive Board and the Pacific, since no extra cost would be  incurred.
10.11 Mr President, Tonga would also like to echo the appeal by the Honourable Minister of the Cook  Islands, in his address to the plenary last week, for UNESCO support for the Pacific Festival of Arts in 1992.  Through the Participation Programme, UNESCO has been responsive to requests made by Pacific Member  States for financial assistance. But considering the importance of this four-yearly pan-Pacific event to the  survival and development of Pacific cultures, we would like to request a more substantial commitment from  UNESCO.
10.12 Mr President, distinguished delegates, I would also like to request your support for the draft resolution  submitted by a number of Pacific Island delegates on a study of the great searoads of the Pacific entitled 'Vaka  Moana’. This cultural project is expected to involve a major collective effort on the part of our island countries  in response to the challenge of the World Decade for Cultural Development. Our view 1s that it is analogous in  scope and significance to UNESCO's General Hi f Africa.
10.13 Mr President, Tonga would like to renew its pledge and commitment to the goals of our Organization.  True, we are a very small country in a far and remote corner of Oceania, but we have not been a passive  observer of the changing world order. Like the global climatic changes that threaten all human beings in all  parts of the world, all changes - whether political, economic, educational, scientific or cultural - have sent
574   ripples and fears throughout the Pacific world. And I would remind this gathering that Kiribati, Tuvalu and my  country, Tonga, are either sinking already or at the mercy of a single tidal wave.
10.14 Despite all the problems that we are now facing, we are none the less optimistic, Mr President, and  believe that what we are suffering are no more than the necessary if sometimes distressing labour pains of the  birth of a new century. We also have faith in the resilience of the human soul and the ability of men and women  to shape their world. In order to enhance this ability to shape a better world, we believe that UNESCO, a  human organization dedicated to the task of developing what is best in humankind, has a vital role to play.
10.15 Thank you, Mr President, and I wish you all the best during your term of office. Malo Aupito. May  God bless you all.

(12.1) Mr BOTTO (Latin Union) (Translation from the Spanish):?  Mr President of the twenty-sixth session of the General Conference of UNESCO, please accept our  congratulations on your election to this high office of presiding over the discussions of this historic cultural forum.
(12.2) Distinguished ministers and ambassadors, members of missions accredited to this Conference,  Mr Director-General of UNESCO, ladies and gentlemen, we are speaking here on behalf of a noble idea, the  Latin Union, an intergovernmental organization whose foundation dates from the approval, on 15 May 1954, of  our Constitution (Treaty of Madrid), the provisions of which merit our profound intellectual respect. We should  like to evoke the memory of Pierre Cabanes, the champion of a noble cause - Latinity. We express our admiration  in regard to the ideas of Jodo Neves da Fontoura, the distinguished head of diplomacy in Itamarati, who presided  over the first congress held in 1951, in Rio de Janeiro. We also call to mind the lucid intellectual qualities of  Paulo de Berrédo Carneiro, Rodolfo Barén Castro, Emilio de Navasqués, the admirable rectors of the Spanish  universities, and Pedro Lain Entralgo, member of the Spanish Academy. We also express our gratitude for the  work carried out by the delegates of France and by the renowned Americanist and friend, Robert de Billy, who  died recently in Paris. We are also grateful for the endeavours of an illustrious colleague, Gianfranco Pompei, a  distinguished Italian diplomat. We pay tribute to Vittorino Veronese, once Director-General of UNESCO. May  we mention our distinguished compatriot, Ambassador Benjamin Fernandez y Medina and extend warm greetings  to Héctor Gros Espiell, at present serving Uruguay, my country, as Chancellor, and of whose enlightened action  the international political scene has abundant proof. We may recall that he was a member of my country's  delegation which signed the Treaty of Madrid in 1954. May we also express our highest consideration and  intellectual esteem to the Uruguayan Minister of Education and Culture, Dr Guillermo Garcia Costa.
(12.3) Our intergovernmental organization will shortly be celebrating its 40 years of existence and this  accordingly calls for an evaluation of its action. Our basis is our Constitution and we must ensure that we do not  depart in any way from its letter or spirit. It makes no provision for agencies or offices abroad, that is to say,  branches established on the territory of the Member States. Such field offices are at variance with legal provisions  and are a costly burden for the Member States and for our institution. In 1954 delegates were far-sighted enough  to create the so-called national commissions, which resembled the National Commissions for UNESCO. The aim  was for the organs of the Latin Union to establish dialogue with distinguished citizens who are specialists in the  various areas of knowledge.
(124) We do not have the monopoly of any supreme moral authority that would serve as a basis for  establishing units not provided for under the law within sovereign States. Respect for the Member States implies  that what is required is intellectual interaction with distinguished specialists in each of the countries concerned  and not some kind of high-level administrative guidance in technical and cultural knowledge. The national  commissions should be set up as soon as possible so as to foster relations involving the theme of Latinity.
(12.5) We are commemorating the Five-Hundredth Anniversary of the Encounter between Two Worlds and it  is hardly conceivable that inequalities should exist among the Member States of our America in the domain of  information. We should call upon the national commissions to promote plans based on the principle of equality  within the context of a lofty dialogue in which contemporary issues could be freely discussed. Everything must be  simpler and more communicative in spirit, without ceasing to be academic. As it nears the end of a long voyage  across the world's oceans, the Latin Union ship has been drifting away from its plotted course. We have, then, to  correct our route if we are to arrive safely in port. It should be pointed out, by way of illustration, that the national  commissions found their origin in the Brazilian National Commission which was entrusted with the organization  of the 1951 international congress, a tribute to the Latinity of the New World, under the presidency of Neves da  Fontoura, then Minister for Foreign Affairs and President of the Brazilian National Commission.
(12.6) In its Articles XV, XX and XXI, the Constitution sets out clearly the Latin Union's working relations  with the national commissions. The Latin Union and the policy of Latinity cannot be divorced. Changes cannot  therefore be introduced in the Preamble to the Constitution, any more than they can be in that of the Charter of  the United Nations. But questions have been raised in international circles in connection with the projected  Secretariat offices in the field. Who defines and directs the policy of Latinity throughout the world? The  Secretariat's branches in the Member States? This would not be logical for ultimately clashes might arise between  the political will of a Member State and an executive office exercising rights at variance with the decisions and  norms of the State in question.
(12.7) For some time now (since March 1978) we have been suggesting the convening of a congress of  Latinity, and the Latin Union's dynamic and distinguished President, the Ambassador of Portugal to UNESCO,  José Augusto Seabra, has just announced that it will definitely be held within a reasonable period of time. Our  objective, if T may express this in personal terms, is the following: we want, in this event, eminent academics,  rectors of the highest integrity, wise professors, learned sociologists, highly qualified authors, socially committed    The speaker provided the Secretariat with the full text of his statement which, with the authorization of  the President, is reproduced in full in annex to this verbatim record.
15   philosophers, acclaimed speakers, grammarians who are the masters of good usage, inspired statisticians, an  upright judiciary, committed educators, determined research workers and the artists of a second Renaissance - we  want them all to make their views known on Latinity and the Latin Union.
(12.8) Ladies and gentlemen, I have referred to the names and qualities of some who are absent and some who  are present among us. It would take too long to depict the wide range of their intellectual attainments with the  same degree of accuracy and discernment shown by those who have sculpted the frieze of Latinity. I pay tribute to  the memory of those who have departed and extend the hand of friendship to all those who argue the caus. ~f  Latinity.
(12.9) The Latin Union is undertaking various programmes concerning the defence and effective use © neo-  Latin languages and this is excellent. Similarly it is taking up subjects such as information technolo; and  cultural communication. Some projects involve lengthy feasibility studies whose funding is costly.
(12.10) We were all deeply grieved at the death in April this year of Ambassador Guillermo Putzeys Alvarez, a  very distinguished intellectual and former President of the Latin Union. His attachment to the theme of Latinity  and his devotion to a set of flawless beliefs earned him international recognition His contribution to UNESCO  and to the Latin Union reflected a lofty humanist philosophy of abiding relevance.

They are eminent world citizens, a fact which  strengthens confidence in our institution for the future.
(12.12) We should like to express one final word of profound conviction. Our defence rests on legal provisions.  It is therefore in the context of our Constitution that we find a way forward marked by clear choices. I reaffirm my  faith, conviction and confidence in UNESCO's ideals and pay tribute to its work in support of the imperishable  values of universal man. Many thanks.
13. The PRESIDENT:  I thank the representative of the Latin Union, and I now give the floor to Mr Jan Erdtsieck, of the  International Federation of Free Teachers' Unions.
14,1 Mr ERDTSIECK (International Federation of Free Teachers’ Unions):  Mr President, distinguished delegates, ladies and gentlemen, I address you today on behalf of some
10 million members, united in 110 educational trade unions throughout the world.
14.2 A democratic country will have not only a plurality of political parties but also a plurality of free and  independent trade unions. The educational trade unions occupy a somewhat unusual position among the latter.  The vast majority of their members are paid directly or indirectly by the government or, if you wish, the State  so the government is almost always the employer of our members. As a result, the four international  educational trade unions - WCOTP, FISE, WCT and my own organization, IFFTU - occupy an unusual  position as NGOs within the framework of UNESCO. UNESCO is an organization of countries and their  governments. In other words, at least in our experience, it is first of all an employers’ organization of  governments employing civil servants and teachers. I am here on behalf of 10 million employees for whom you  are, directly or indirectly, the employer. Ten million members in 110 national educational trade unions, and we  have only five minutes to speak. This is maybe a sign of the contempt that still exists for trade unions. Despite  the fact that the [LO-UNESCO Recommendation on the status of teachers contains ringing phrases on the  rights of teachers and their trade unions, there is very little to show today, 25 years after the acceptance of the  Recommendation. It is still the case that governments in many countries with an authoritarian regime trample  the Recommendation under foot, while educational union officials and members are unjustly imprisoned,  prevented from carrying out their work properly, or sec their rights curtailed. In other countries only a single  trade union is allowed, controlled by the State or the party. I have mentioned these appalling situations on  previous occasions in referring to numerous publications in this respect by Amnesty International and other  human rights organizations. 1 shall not tire you again with the long list of countries in which the  Recommendation is nothing more than a scrap of paper.
14.3 Although the relationship between the four international educational unions and the Education Sector  of UNESCO is very good in the informal context, and in this respect I would like to thank Mr Power and his
578   staff, there is still no recognition of our special position by those who wield decision-making power in  UNESCO and other United Nations organizations. I would like to illustrate this point with a few examples.
144 Changes in the educational system always affect the legal status and working conditions of the teacher.  Consequently, whatever the nature of the changes to be introduced in education may be, in all cases and at all  levels they demand due and serious consultations and negotiations with the teachers and their unions before  being implemented. Though these principles are often accepted in theory, they arc seldom followed in practice,  as is patently obvious. In every civilized country there is a structure for collective bargaining between the  government and the educational trade unions on a basis of mutual equality. This basis should exist not only at  the national level, but also at the international level, i.e. within the framework of UNESCO.
14.5 Bearing this in mind, it would have been only natural to invite the four international educational  unions to speak at the official celebration of the twenty-fifth anniversary of the Recommendation, so that the  voice of those who provide education within the school and classroom could be heard. We, however, for reasons  unknown to us, were not given the chance. This contempt for teachers and their trade union organization finds  an even stronger expression - in the composition of the educational forum, which will assess the first  implementation of the Jomtien decisions and take initiatives to ensure that the worldwide campaign for  education for all is finally won by the year 2000. So far we see no signs that this target will be achieved, but in  any case it will certainly be unattainable without the co-operation of classroom teachers and their unions. It was  through an informal channel that we discovered that this forum is to consist of 50 members, eight of them to be  chosen from eight different NGOs. We do not know which NGOs these are, nor the criteria for their selection  for the forum, but we have been informed that only one delegate will be allowed to represent the combination of  the four international educational unions. Qur protest at such an unacceptable failure to appreciate our position  was met with a brief but unambiguous ‘take it or leave it. There is only one conclusion possible: a good
40 million teachers are palmed off with a single representative in a forum of 50 delegates whose function is to  supervise the attainment of an objective which will remain a mirage without the co-operation of the teachers  themselves. We appeal to you and to your influence to prevent this act of injustice from being perpetrated. Do  not forget that a sensible employer knows that the best way of attaining his objectives is to have motivated  employees with whom he can negotiate on the basis of equality, as both parties recognize their joint  responsibility.
14.6 — As I said, it is already now obvious that the ambitious objective of Jomtien will certainly not be  attained by the year 2000, despite the unfounded optimism and euphoria which prevailed at Jomtien. The  pre-conditions for success which were lacking then are still lacking today. As long as education comes at the  bottom of the national budget as an item which can be slashed to one's heart's content, as in so many countries;  as long as unqualified staff provide low-quality education for ridiculously low wages, as in so many countries;  as long as the status of teachers is drastically undervalued, as in so many countries; as long as governments are  more keen on spending money on arms to oppress their own people or ethnic minorities or to threaten  neighbouring countries than on building schools; as long as this situation continues, the attainment of basic  education for all, at least if it is to mean anything more than a minimal ability to read, write and count, will  remain a pious dream. We want nothing more than to transform the ideals of Jomtien into reality, but this calls  for a profound change of mentality as a first step towards the political willingness to eventually recognize the  importance of quality basic education for all. This change of mentality will never take place without the active  and loyal support of the teachers. Therefore: recognize the value of the educational profession in accordance  with the Recommendation. The time has come to apply this in a broad-minded way. Only then will there be a  chance of achieving the goals of Jomtien, if not within a decade, then perhaps within 25 years. ! thank you.
15. The PRESIDENT:  I thank the representative of the International Federation of Free Teachers’ Unions. Our next speaker is  H.E. Mr Lambert Messan, Ambassador, Permanent Delegate and head of the delegation of Niger.
16.1 M. MESSAN (Niger) :  Monsieur le Président de la Conférence générale, Monsieur le Président du Conseil exécutif, Monsieur  le Directeur général, Mesdames et Messieurs les chefs de délégation, Mesdames et Messieurs les délégués, vous  me permettrez, Monsieur le Président, de vous présenter tout d'abord les félicitations de ma délégation pour  votre brillante élection à la présidence de la Conférence générale. Cet hommage à votre personne et, à travers  vous, au Kenya et à l'Afrique, nous va droit au coeur. En effet, en vous portant à ces hautes fonctions, la  Conférence générale a voulu appeler à sa présidence quinze ans après Nairobi, un représentant de l'Afrique, ce  dont nous nous réjouissons tout particulièrement. À S. Exc. M. Anwar Ibrahim, nous rendons un vibrant
15   hommage pour la manière dont il a dirigé la précédente session. La compétence et les qualités de notre  Président du Conseil exécutif, S. Exc. M. Yahya Aliyu, ainsi que de tous les autres membres du Conseil,  méritent d'être soulignées. Grâce à leur clairvoyance d'esprit et aux qualités intellectuelles et morales dont ils  ont fait preuve, un consensus a pu être trouvé sur toutes les propositions qui nous sont soumises. C'est la raison  pour laquelle nous apportons notre appui au Projet de programme et de budget pour l'exercice 1992-1993, nous  réservant néanmoins d'intervenir en commission sur quelques points spécifiques qui nous intéressent  particulièrement. Monsieur le Directeur général, je ne puis résister au plaisir de rendre un hommage mérité à  vos qualités qui ont permis, en concertation avec le Conseil exécutif, de donner un souffle nouveau à  l'Organisation par des réformes judicicuses de fonctionnement et de gestion. Cette tâche doit être poursuivie  ainsi que les réformes de structure dans un esprit de rationalisation, certes, mais aussi d'équilibre et d'efficacité.
16,2 Monsieur le Président, depuis la fin de la vingt-cinquième session de la Conférence générale, la  situation internationale a été profondément modifiée par de nombreux paramètres socioculturels, reflets des  contradictions et de l'accentuation des inégalités. En cherchant, d'une part, à extrapoler à partir des nombreuses  crises nationales et internationales qui se sont succédé et qui se poursuivent, crises alimentées par de puissants  moyens de communication et de transport ou, d'autre part, à tout expliquer à partir de schémas théoriques ou de  critères strictement quantitatifs, on limite la compréhension d'un monde en pleine mutation. Il faut pousser plus  loin l'analyse.
16.3 En effet que constatons-nous ? Une accélération sans précédent de l'histoire avec une progression  démographique exponentielle, accentuant encore plus les risques d'implosion liés à l'analphabétisme et à la  misère ; une dégradation croissante de l'environnement qui fait craindre le pire pour la survie de l'humanité si  des mesures ne sont pas prises à temps pour préserver notre planète ; une revendication identitaire des groupes  ethniques et linguistiques, comprimée jusque-là de diverses façons, au mépris des libertés et de la dignité  humaine ; un déséquilibre structurel à l'échelon du monde, à l'intérieur des sociétés, entre le Nord et le Sud,  mais également entre l'Ouest et l'Est ; une internationalisation de l'action des individus qui nous fait assister à  des mutations d'attitudes, d'habitudes et d'aspirations qui bouleversent les comportements en matière de  consommation, de loisirs, de travail, de tourisme, d'immigration, de santé, d'éducation à l'échelle du monde.  Par la globalisation et l'interdépendance, l'univers se rétrécit et s'élargit en même temps, la planète est  structurée, limitée, mais l'univers spatial, océanique et intellectuel s'agrandit sans cesse. Les mondes des loisirs,  de la culture suivent également le même rythme. Les connaissances et le savoir deviennent de plus en plus  sources de travail, de richesse par rapport aux matières premières et aux biens manufacturés. Dans un tel  contexte, il faut trouver les voies et moyens capables de faire prendre collectivement conscience aux  gouvernements, aux femmes et aux hommes de pensée, des enjeux les plus profonds de cette mutation  planétaire. Notre Organisation est, semble-t-il, seule capable, de par sa nature et sa vocation, comme l'indique  l'Acte constitutif, de jouer pleinement ce rôle.
16.4 Monsieur le Président, c'est pourquoi la première des priorités, à notre avis, est que tout individu ait  libre accès au savoir. Nous ne pouvons par conséquent qu'être extrêmement préoccupés par le taux  d'analphabétisme dans le monde en général et plus particulièrement dans les pays les moins avancés dont fait  malheureusement partie mon pays. Nous nous félicitons des décisions de la Conférence de Jomtien sur  l'éducation pour tous et aussi de la priorité accordée à ce secteur comme au continent africain dans le Projet de  programme et de budget qui nous est proposé.
16.5 Pour un pays qui se demande à chaque saison ce que sera demain et qui voit le déséquilibre de son  écosystème, les problèmes d'environnement prennent une autre dimension. Aussi, ne pouvons-nous  qu'encourager tous les efforts visant à permettre à l'humanité de vivre en harmonie avec son environnement et  nous apprécions hautement la prochaine inscription de l'Aïr et du Ténéré sur la Liste du patrimoine mondial  culturel et naturel de l'UNESCO. Nous lançons dès maintenant un appel à la communauté internationale pour  la préservation et le développement de ce site, qui a conquis tous ceux qui ont pu le découvrir. À cet égard, le  rendez-vous du mois de juin prochain à Rio, où vont être définis les enjeux en matère d'environnement et  adopté un plan d'action pour mettre en oeuvre un développement durable de la planète, sera une épreuve  capitale pour notre Organisation qui se doit de bien la préparer.
16.6 = Monsieur le Président, nous sommes heureux de voir les progrès réalisés à l'égard de la démocratie et  de la promotion des droits de l'homme, de la femme, des minorités, des enfants et de tous les exclus. Mais la  fragilité de ces acquis fait que nous insistons particulièrement pour que l'UNESCO affirme davantage sa place  et son rôle dans les activités liées à ces secteurs. Nous félicitons le Directeur général pour ses efforts et ses  initiatives dans ces domaines ; nous l'encourageons à les poursuivre et à donner l'exemple en ouvrant avec
580   équité les structures de direction du Secrétariat de l'UNESCO, afin que les intentions soient traduites en actes.  Le nombre des femmes, premiers vecteurs de la culture et de l'éducation, est encore trop faible. À notre avis, il  en est de même de certaines couches défavorisées, exclues si souvent de l'accès au savoir et, par conséquent, de  toutes les possibilités d'intégration sociale dont devrait bénéficier tout individu.
16.7 Monsieur le Président, pour ce qui est de la communication, il convient de constater que, grâce aux  efforts faits par le Directeur général et ses collaborateurs, de réels progrès ont été accomplis pour rendre lcs  actions de l'UNESCO plus visibles et pour permettre l'éclosion d'une presse plus libre, indépendante et  pluraliste dans les pays en pleine mutation politique.
