48 governments and four other benefactors totalled US $227 million of which US $210 million have been  received already. However, in spite of these contributions and Japan's initial generous contribution of  US $100 million to the Endowment Fund, we are still short of the US $500 million dreamed as a target when  the University was established some 15 years ago.
30.13 Fund-raising is thus a continuous challenge for the University, as it considers expansion and co-  operation with others. We are very thankful to the 48 countries that have contributed to UNU, and in particular,  we welcome the recent substantial pledges received from the Governments of Macao, Portugal and the People's  Republic of China. I am also very grateful to the Governments of the Netherlands and Finland, which have  made significant contributions to the UNU Endowment Fund. This is not the moment and the place to thank  each and every donor country. But special thanks, Mr President, should go to those countries with serious  economic difficulties such as Brazil, Nigeria, Colombia, Zambia, Egypt and Ghana, which have pledged and  paid substantial contributions in terms of their respective GNP in hard currency to our Endowment and  Operating Funds. We are still halfway to the initial target set by the founding fathers, but we are very confident  that we shall get there as we progress in academic performance and in institutional vitality.
30.14 I would like to close this statement, Mr President, by expressing to all of you my deepest gratitude for  your continued support for the University's work and to me personally, at the time when I am starting my  second term in office.
30.15 In particular, I would like to thank the Director-General, Mr Federico Mayor, for honouring me with  his constant encouragement and sustained support, and all his colleagues at UNESCO for their co-operation  with the United Nations University. Thank you.  (M. Edjo Ovono, Guinée équatoriale, prend la présidence)
(31.1) The PRESIDENT (Translation from the Spanish}:  I thank the Rector of the United Nations University. Mr Cherif, President of the World Islamic Call  Society now has the floor.

(32.1) Mr CHERIF (World Islamic Call Society) (Translation from the Arabic):  In the name of God, the Compassionate, the Merciful. Blessings and peace be on his Messenger.  Mr President, it gives me great pleasure to congratulate you on your election to the presidency of this Conference.  I should also like to commend the Director-General and his colleagues in the Secretariat for their efforts in  administering the affairs of this Organization.
(32.2) Distinguished delegates, on behalf of the Islamic Call Society and its members throughout the world, I  would like to renew our expression of confidence in UNESCO and in its efforts to promote understanding through  culture, education and science so that all peoples may come to know each other in an atmosphere of mutual  respect, The Quran, the holy book of Islam, that great religion whose high humanitarian values our Organization  seeks to propagate, declares as follows: '0 mankind! We created you from a single (pair) of a male and a female,  and made you into nations and tribes, that you may know each other. Verily the most honoured of you in the sight  of God is (he who is) the most righteous of you'.
(32.3) Since its establishment in Tripoli, the capital of the Libyan Arab Jamahiriya, by the grace of God and  with the goodwill of Colonel Muammar Qaddafi, the leader of the September Revolution, the Islamic Call  Society, thanks to the generosity of the Libyan people, has devoted its labours to the affirmation of these high  humanitarian principles. Its 21-year record of civic action has included the establishment of hundreds of schools,  university colleges, hospitais, dispensaries, homes and refuges, orphanages, public libraries, language institutes  and technical schools - first in Africa and Asia and later in Europe and the Americas. Its activities have included  assistance to thousands of students in all fields of knowledge and know-how and to scientists doing field work in  medicine, agriculture and other areas that serve human development and progress. It has also assisted in the  publishing of millions of copies of magazines, books, newspapers and other publications which transmit more  knowledge to the people. An exhibition will be opened next Friday to illustrate these activities. The Society has  encouraged co-operation with all international, scholarly and religious institutions in the establishment of a  constructive scholarly and cultural dialogue in the service of all that is humane and conducive to progress.
(32.4) The Society is strongly attached to its programmes of co-operation with UNESCO in the fields of  science, education and culture. I want to take this opportunity to thank the Director-General, Mr Federico Mayor,  and his assistants for all they are doing to strengthen the ties of co-operation between UNESCO and the Society in  our mutual interest and in the service of constructive human values.
(32.5) Ladies and gentlemen, my relationship with UNESCO, whose mission is to serve the good of all  humanity, is a source of personal gratification. The pioneering programmes that have been carried out by  scientists and experts under the aegis of this Organization are a matter of historical record. Cases in point that  come to mind at once include the genesis at UNESCO of the projects for the building of a new world economic  order, a new world information and communication order and a new international order. These universalist  projects were an attempt to give expression to all the cultures of this world, which has shrunk to the point where
15   it must now be regarded as the common property of a humanity in which no single culture may dominate another.  These projects, however, were opposed by certain powers which regarded them as mere fantasies dreamed up by  the weak and destitute peoples of the Third World. And now we hear a lot of talk about a so-called new world  order, an attractive slogan which has become current, but whose content remains obscure, peculiar and mercurial.  It contains some puzzling features and sounds very much like an invitation to the peoples of the world to submit  to the hegemony of a certain culture and region over the destiny of all mankind.
(32.6) All cultures deserve respect; in Palestine however, children do not receive a proper education because  they live under an oppressive regime. There is a feeling in the Islamic world that an effort is under way to destroy  the Al-Aqsa Mosque, the symbol of a spiritual and humanitarian culture whose importance transcends Islam. I  should like to assure you that our Society is eager to co-operate with UNESCO in the task of building a genuine  new world to which all peoples would contribute in a spirit of democracy and through a cultural programme that  is open to everyone. I wish you God's peace, mercy and blessings.
(33) The PRESIDENT (Translation from the Spanish):  I thank the President of the World Islamic Call Society. Ms Moreau, Chairwoman of the Standing  Committee of International Non-Governmental Organizations now has the floor.
34.1 Mme MOREAU (Comité permanent des organisations internationales non gouvernementales) :  Monsieur le Président, Monsieur le Directeur général, Mesdames et Messieurs les chefs de délégation,  Mesdames et Messieurs les délégués, "l'avenir de la coopération internationale dépend des nouveaux  partenariats, du renforcement des liens entre institutions partageant les mêmes buts et les mêmes rêves. Et vous  jouez un rôle de premier plan dans cette action commune compétitive et efficace”. Ainsi s'exprimait le  Directeur général lorsqu'il s'est adressé aux ONG à l'occasion de la vingt-deuxième Conférence des ONG, tenue  en juin de l'année dernière. Le rapport sexennal du Conseil exécutif sur le concours apporté à l'action de  l'UNESCO par les ONG, présenté à la vingt-cinquième session de la Conférence générale, rappelait que "la  participation des organisations internationales non gouvernementales à l'oeuvre de l'UNESCO constitue un  moyen privilégié pour l'Organisation d'associer les peuples à son oeuvre, particulièrement les milieux  intellectuels et scientifiques compétents, de même que les mouvements représentatifs de l'opinion publique  concernés par le développement de l'éducation, la science, la culture et la communication dans le monde." La  place des ONG dans le cadre de l'UNESCO est donc majeure en raison de l'importance croissante prise partout  dans le monde par la société civile, dont l'expression et le rôle dans les sociétés ont pu connaître un  développement sans précédent par suite de la démocratisation de nombreuses sociétés dans le monde.
34.2  Malgré ce soutien apporté tant par le Directeur général que par de nombreux Etats membres, et je tiens  ici à les en remercier tout particulièrement, j'ai pris note du souhait exprimé par certains Etats membres de voir  les ONG améliorer encore leur extension géographique, de revoir la politique d'octroi des subventions et de  mieux tenir compte des besoins qui s'expriment dans les pays en développement. Mais si les ONG doivent  consentir des efforts supplémentaires à l'avenir, il semble que certaines appréciations d'Etats membres à leur  égard procèdent parfois d'un manque d'information sur ce que sont les ONG de caractère international et  régional. Sans doute sont-elles responsables de ce manque d'information.
343 Qui sont-elles ? Elles représentent des millions de personnes qui travaillent et luttent pour atteindre les  mêmes objectifs et les mêmes idéaux que ceux des Etats membres et qui ne sont autres que ceux de l'UNESCO,  c'est-à-dire être au service du développement des hommes. Ce que les ONG sollicitent lorsqu'elles demandent  de l'aide au titre du Programme de participation est destiné au bénéfice des mêmes populations que celles qui  sont aidées par les Etats membres. Qui, mieux que les ONG, peut atteindre toutes les couches de ces  populations, avec un effet démultiplicateur incontestable, et ceci dans tous les domaines de compétence de  l'UNESCO ? J'aimerais aussi attirer l'attention sur le fait qu'au sein de ces ONG oeuvrent des milliers de  personnes à titre purement bénévole, au service des plus démunis, et si l'on devait évaluer cette contribution en  termes financiers, il n'est pas difficile de saisir que les 3 % du budget de l'UNESCO alloués aux ONG ne sont  qu'une goutte d'eau en comparaison, Notre souhait est que lorsque viendra l'examen de cette question, les Etats  membres tiennent compte de ce potentiel que représentent les ONG et que la Conférence générale adopte le  projet de résolution concernant le Programme de participation tel que proposé par le Directeur général dans le  Projet de programme et de budget.
344  L'exigence de l'extension géographique est souvent rappelée à propos des ONG internationales ayant  leur siège dans l'hémisphère Nord, Ces ONG comptent pour la plupart des membres dans un grand nombre de  pays situés dans les différentes régions du monde et sont animées par des personnalités élues selon des critères  de répartition géographique. Elles s'efforcent, dans toute la mesure du possible, de tenir leurs activités dans ces  différentes régions et de les intensifier. Mais c'est pour mieux exercer leur mandat qu'elles ont souvent choisi  d'avoir leur siège à proximité des institutions des Nations Unies avec lesquelles elles coopèrent le plus. Or, tout  comme pour l'UNESCO, leur principe est celui de l'universalité. Certes existe-il des ONG dans le Nord et dans  le Sud, et sans doute l'UNESCO doit y être attentive, mais il importe d'observer que le modèle d'ONG auquel  elle a voulu s'associer et qui est défini dans les Directives adoptées par la Conférence générale elle-même, est  un modèle de coopération multilatérale à l'image de l'UNESCO, où les personnes et les groupes d'Afrique,  d'Asie, d'Amérique latine, d'Europe et d'Amérique du Nord, peuvent coopérer dans le respect des identités  culturelles, compte tenu du principe de l'égalité de droit reconnue à chacun.
345 Le Directeur général a utilisé le mot "partenariat". Tel est en effet le souhait des ONG : être des  partenaires à part entière avec les exigences qu'implique cette forme de coopération. Etre des partenaires pour  l'UNESCO, pour les Etats membres et pour les commissions nationales. À plusieurs reprises, des représentants  d'Etats membres ont soulevé la question de la coopération avec les ONG et exprimé le désir que celle-ci  s'intensifie. C'est aussi le souhait des ONG, qui demandent que l'UNESCO facilite ces relations. Et je voudrais  ici proposer que, lors des réunions régionales des commissions nationales où le Comité permanent des ONG est  toujours invité - et j'en remercie les organisateurs - un point soit systématiquement inscrit à cet effet à l'ordre du  jour. Cela permettrait au représentant du Comité permanent de prendre part à ce dialogue tant souhaité.
34.6 Ce partenariat se concrétise par l'action collective menée par les ONG internationales dans la mise en  oeuvre du programme de l'UNESCO. Au sein même du Comité permanent, des groupes de travail coopèrent  souvent étroitement avec le Secrétariat. Parmi ceux-ci un groupe se penche plus particulièrement sur la  condition de la femme, la famille, les droits de l'homme, sciences et éthique, la communication et  l'environnement. L'alphabétisation et l'éducation des adultes ainsi que la jeunesse - autres priorités du  programme de l'UNESCO - sont traitées au sein de deux consultations collectives très actives, la Consultation  collective UNESCO/ONG sur l'alphabétisation et l'éducation des adultes et la Consultation collective  UNESCO/ONG sur la jeunesse. Le travail qui s'y fait est important car il débouche sur des projets concrets.
34,7 Le suivi de la Conférence de Jomtien sur l'éducation pour tous est un autre domaine dans lequel les  ONG se mobilisent pour coopérer à la mise en oeuvre du Cadre d'action. À l'initiative des Comités d'ONG  dotées du statut consultatif auprès des quatre institutions des Nations Unies ayant parrainé la Conférence, et en  coopération avec elles, un réseau se met en place pour permettre, entre autres ‘choses, la diffusion de  l'information dans un esprit d'échange continu. L'objectif de ce réseau est d'attendre les ONG qui travaillent à  la base, qu'elles soient locales, nationales, régionales ou internationales.
348 A ce stade, j'aimerais vous dire combien nous nous réjouissons de l'esprit de dialogue qui règne  actuellement au sein du Comité des ONG du Conseil exécutif ; les ONG ont été, en effet, appelées à traiter des  questions qui les concernent avec la possibilité de s'exprimer et de présenter leurs vues.
34.9 Je voudrais terminer en soulignant l'attachement des ONG aux idéaux de l'UNESCO, dont la mission  en tant que gardienne et promotrice d'une éthique et d'une morale au sein de la communauté mondiale leur  paraît irremplaçable. Merci, Monsieur le Président.
(35) The PRESIDENT (Translation from the Spanish):  I thank the Chairwoman of the Standing Committee of International Non-Governmental Organizations. I  now call upon Ms Blanchin, representative of the Collective Consultation of Non-Governmental Youth  Organizations.
36.1 Mlle BLLANCHIN (Consultation collective des organisations non gouvernementales de jeunesse) :  Monsieur le Président, Monsieur le Directeur général, Excellences, Mesdames, Messieurs, "La  jeunesse est ici chez elle, à l'UNESCO". Cette phrase fut prononcée 1l y a près de 25 ans, devant cette même  Conférence générale par l'un des prédécesseurs de M. Mayor pour répondre aux interrogations soulevées par la  crise de la jeunesse, apparue au cours des années 60.
36.2 Depuis lors, les jeunes générations qui se sont succédé ont pu remarquer que l'Organisation s'est  employée, avec des moyens modestes, dans des structures mouvantes et avec des succès divers, mais avec une  bonne volonté certaine, à développer son action dans ce domaine, en particulier en jouant un rôle moteur pour  l'ensemble du système des Nations Unies dans la célébration de l'Année internationale de la jeunesse, en  convoquant le Congrès mondial sur la jeunesse à Barcelone en 1985, et tout récemment en adoptant le thème  transversal "Jeunesse" et en lançant un projet mobilisateur "La jeunesse, pour façonner l'avenir”. C'est aussi  grâce à l'impulsion de l'UNESCO qu'un groupe interinstitutions sur la jeunesse a été créé au sein du système  des Nations Unies.
36.3 L'originalité et l'impact de l'action ainsi entreprise réside, me semble-t-il, dans le véritable partenariat  qui, au-delà d'une simple coopération, si étroite qu'elle puisse être, s'est progressivement institué entre  l'UNESCO et les organisations internationales non gouvernementales de jeunesse.
36.4 C'est précisément au nom du groupe chargé de la concertation et de la coopération entre ces ONG que  je m'exprime aujourd'hui devant vous. Ce mécanisme de consultation collective, mis en place par l'UNESCO, et  qui vient de tenir sa 17e session le mois dernier en Roumanie, rassemble près de 80 organisations et constitue le  réseau associatif peut-être le plus important existant dans le monde. C'est au Directeur général et à son  Secrétariat qu'il appartient de valoriser ce réseau, comme cela a été fait le mois dernier en Roumanie, en  orientant son action et son influence de manière à en faire un instrument de plus en plus efficace au service des  objectifs de l'Organisation et destiné à promouvoir la vie associative des jeunes.
36.5 La jeunesse a la réputation d'être téméraire. Est-ce justifier cette réputation que de demander que  l'UNESCO se donne, dans le domaine de la jeunesse, les moyens de ses ambitions ? Pour illustrer mon propos,  je prendrai l'exemple des publications et des campagnes d'information de l'UNESCO qui ne s'adressent  qu'exceptionnellement aux jeunes, qui ne sont généralement pas conçues pour eux ni avec eux. L'Organisation,  comme cela a été fait récemment avec des jeunes journalistes, doit se donner la possibilité et les moyens d'agir  sur cette composante essentielle de l'opinion publique qu'est l'opinion, multiforme il est vrai, de la jeunesse.
36.6 Bien d'autres questions me viennent à l'esprit qui me conduiraient à déborder largement le temps qui  m'est imparti. Pourquoi, néanmoins, le recrutement du personnel ne s'applique-t-il pas davantage à répondre à  la nécessité, pourtant évidente, d'associer les jeunes générations à l'oeuvre entreprise ? Le pourcentage de jeunes  et particulièrement de jeunes femmes demeure faible dans le Secrétariat de l'UNESCO. Nous comptons tous sur  la détermination annoncée par le Directeur général pour que cette situation soit améliorée. La jeunesse doit être  vue, ainsi que le Directeur général l'a souligné à plusieurs reprises, comme un élément moteur de la réforme et  de la renaissance de l'UNESCO.
36.7 Monsieur le Président, la jeunesse n'est pas seulement téméraire, elle a aussi la réputation d'être  idéaliste, encore que le qualificatif demanderait à être nuancé et explicité. Je ne sais si je confirmerai cette  réputation en rappelant devant votre assemblée que c'est dans l'esprit des jeunes que doivent s'enraciner les  valeurs et les idéaux qui sont à la base de l'humanisme moderne et que l'UNESCO a pour mandat et pour  mission de propager. La jeunesse ne constitue pas seulement un segment, aussi important soit-il, de la  population des différents pays du monde, un groupe hétérogène et par définition transitoire pris volontiers pour  cible et pour support par les médias, les propagandes ou la publicité. Elle est d'abord un état d'esprit. Elle est  surtout ce qui apparaît comme l'instrument peut-être le plus précieux dans la lutte pour sa survie que doit  conduire aujourd'hui l'humanité, confrontée à des problèmes globaux qu'aucune nation, si puissante soit-elle, ne  peut affronter isolément : je veux dire l'espérance.
36.8 C'est au nom de cette espérance que la jeunesse non seulement réclame d'être chez elle à l'UNESCO,  mais qu'elle y revendique la place, toute la place, au plan conceptuel, programmatique, budgétaire ou  administratif, qui doit lui revenir. Laissez-moi vous dire ma conviction qu'il y va de l'intérêt et de l'avenir de  notre Organisation que de la lui reconnaître.
36.9 Je vous souhaite, Monsieur le Directeur général, le plus grand succès pour l'oeuvre de renouveau que  vous avez entreprise à l'UNESCO, et je tiens à vous assurer de notre soutien.
(37) The PRESIDENT (Translation from the Spanish):  I thank the representative of the Collective Consultation of Non-Governmental Youth Organizations.  Mr Coudreau, representative of the International Catholic Child Bureau now has the floor.
38.1 M. COUDREAU (Bureau international catholique de l'enfance) :  Monsieur le Président, Mesdames, Messieurs, répondant ici même, il y a deux ans, à la proposition du  Directeur général, la Conférence générale de l'UNESCO a inscrit à son programme sexennal pour 1990-1995  un thème nouveau : “Le jeune enfant et le milieu familial”. En cet instant, les enfants tournent les yeux vers  votre Organisation, qui s'engage à les servir.
38.2 — C'est en me référant à ce projet intersectoriel et interdisciplinaire, qu'au nom de mon organisation, le  Bureau international catholique de l'enfance, je souhaite vous faire part de trois soucis : le premier de l'ordre de  l'urgence, le second de l'ordre de la pédagogie et le troisième de l'ordre de l'actualité.
38.3 Le premier, de l'ordre de l'urgence, s'inspire du champ majeur de programme V. Le programme V.2 se  veut attentif "au changement social” pour adapter l'action de l'UNESCO à un "monde en mutation”. L'actuelle  situation tragique de tant d'enfants est un rappel vigoureux de cette urgence. Vous le savez, de terribles fléaux  -la drogue, la prostitution, le sida - sont désormais entrés dans le monde des enfants. Mais ces fléaux  dévastateurs ont une source : la marginalité urbaine (mentionnée dans l'action de programme décrite au  paragraphe 05204), qui a donné naissance au phénomène appelé "les enfants de la rue”, véritable scandale de  notre temps. Selon des statistiques récentes, ce serait 6 à 7 millions d'enfants qu'aujourd'hui la société met "à la  rue” : d'abord marginalisés, puis rejetés, enfin exclus, ils sont condamnés à la dégradation la plus pitoyable et la  plus honteuse. Les actions entreprises au titre du programme V.2 (par. 05204) prennent en compte un aspect de  cette situation intolérable. Mon organisation souhaite que l'UNESCO - c'est le mandat qu'elle a reçu - s'engage  dans la réflexion et l'action pour donner à tous ces enfants de la rue l'éducation appropriée dont ils ont besoin.
384 Note second souci est de l'ordre de la pédagogie. Il fait écho au paragraphe 01208 du champ majeur  de programme I, demandant la création d'une commission de l'éducation pour le XXIe siècle, chargée  d'examiner des méthodes pédagogiques en vue de contribuer à "l'élaboration d'une nouvelle approche de  l'éducation”. En appuyant cette demande, mon organisation voudrait proposer à cette commission un objectif  précis. Comme ne cesse de le répéter le Directeur général, on ne peut plus se contenter de donner aux enfants et  aux jeunes un savoir et de les préparer à une profession. Dans un monde où augmentent les turbulences et les  désarrois, il faut aussi, et peut-être d'abord, leur apprendre à être et à être avec, à être eux-mêmes et à être  ensemble. Il est donc nécessaire de promouvoir une pédagogie de l'autonomie” et de la "relation" permettant  aux enfants de grandir comme personnes humaines conscientes de leur dignité et de devenir aptes à vivre en  communauté. Il y a là un défi à relever pour l'UNESCO.
38.5 — Enfin, notre troisième souci, et j'achève, est de l'ordre de l'actualité. Il s'inspire également du champ  majeur de programme V. Les paragraphes 05205 et 05210 attirent notre attention sur la préparation de l'Année  internationale de la famille. Mon organisation insiste sur le rôle indispensable de la famille pour l'enfant. L'être  humain, pour naître et grandir, a besoin d'une famille. Autant que d'un couple de "géniteurs” qui lui transmette  la vie, l'enfant a besoin d'un couple de parents qui soit pour lui une communauté d'amour. La famille est le sein  maternel de la société où les petits d'homme, véritables embryons de personne humaine, à part entière certes  mais "en devenir", font nid pour s'éveiller à l'amour. La famille est la terre nourricière dans laquelle l'enfant  plonge ses racines pour que puisse germer et s'épanouir la semence de l'amour. Elle est la demeure sacrée,  unique et mystérieuse, où s'accomplit l'enfantement de la dimension spirituelle de l'être humain, c'est-à-dire  l'éclosion et le surgissement de l'amour qui, avec l'intelligence et la liberté, lui confèrent son identité et sa  dignité. C'est pourquoi un enfant sans famille, bien plus que frustré, est un enfant en danger.
38.6 S1 la Conférence accueille favorablement ces trois requêtes - les enfants de la rue, le renouveau de la  pédagogie et la fonction éducatrice de la famille - alors, Mesdames et Messieurs, vos regards, ce soir, auront  réellement rencontré le regard des enfants, de tous les enfants du monde. Puisse cette rencontre sécher leurs  larmes, provoquer leur sourire et déposer en eux le bien le plus précieux, la joie de vivre. Je vous remercie.
(39) The PRESIDENT (Translation from the Spanish):  I thank the representative of the International Catholic Child Bureau and now call upon Mr O'Keefe,  representative of the World Confederation of Organizations of the Teaching Profession.
40.1 Mr O'KEEFE (World Confederation of Organizations of the Teaching Profession, WCOTP):  Mr President and distinguished delegates, as a teacher it is indeed an honour for me to have this  opportunity to speak on behalf of some 14 million teachers and educational workers whose professional  organizations belong to WCOTP. Those organizations, spread worldwide, share my words of greeting and  congratulations to UNESCO on the occasion of this historic session.
40.2 When one celebrates the twenty-fifth anniversary of such an important document as the ILO/UNESCO  Recommendation on the Status of Teachers, there is a natural tendency to look to the past. A time to see  mankind's hopes and dreams. A time to evaluate how well plans were made and carried out and to assess the  positive and negative results of our actions. We realize that we live and work in the present, as we plan for a  better future for all the peoples of planet Earth. We also realize that it would be a grave mistake not to pause to  take this backward glance.
40.3 This week, in my home province of Nova Scotia, Canada, I came into possession of the general  programme of the second Biennial Conference of the World Federation of Education Associations (WFEA).  The date of the Conference was from 7 to 13 August 1927. Allow me to cite from this programme just some of  the themes of concern to that international body, meeting 64 years ago: education for peace; study of methods  and instruments used to settle international disputes without resorting to war; health education programme;  illiteracy division; and social and educational phases of the behavioural problems in young people. In addition,  a special session was held on the topic of the preparation of teachers for international co-operation and  goodwill. We can see that this partial listing of concerns has a direct relationship to many of the programmes of  UNESCO since its founding, and - dare I say - up to the present and into the future as well.
40.4 Mr President, UNESCO cannot and is not expected to carry out all of its vital programmes alone. My  standing and speaking to you today is a recognition of the important role played by non-governmental  organizations (NGOs) in partnership with UNESCO. For its part, WCOTP, together with the three other  teacher internationals, was pleased to play a leading role in two recent conferences on issues of vital concern.  The first, dealing with AIDS, that destroyer of the human body, was held in Paris; and the second, dealing with  literacy, whose absence is a destroyer of the human spirit, was held in Jomtien.
40.5 Iam pleased to report that WCOTP does not consider such conferences to be an end unto themselves.  It is the degree of resultant action that counts. Since these conferences, WCOTP has organized the Panafrican  Conference in Accra, the Caribbean Conference in Nassau, the Latin America Conference in San José as well  as the European workshop in Czechoslovakia. Time does not permit me to list other regions or other member  organization meetings which have addressed these important concems.
40.6 On this, its twenty-fifth anniversary, WCOTP regrets that the leadership role in the area of education  seems to be passing from the hands of its rightful holder, UNESCO, into the waiting grasp of organizations  such as the World Bank, whose aims are based not on educational concerns but on economic policies.
40.7 Again, on this twenty-fifth anniversary, WCOTP regrets that many recommendations have been so  badly applied - if applied at all - in certain regions, particularly in far too many of the Latin American  countries.
40.8 And yet again, on this twenty-fifth anniversary, WCOTP views with dismay the consequences of  deprofessionalization, demotivation, increased assaults on the trade union rights of teachers, on their  professional rights, and even negation - in some quarters - of academic freedoms.
40.9 Mr President, I wish to make it very clear that all of the above are not problems exclusive to the  developing nations of the world. As a case in point, my own government in Nova Scotia has recently broken its  signed collective agreement with the Nova Scotia Teachers Union and has also legislated a two-year freeze on  all collective bargaining with its public sector workers. The shame of such unilateral action can be found in an  ever-increasing number of locations.
40.10 WCOTP calls on UNESCO to defend and follow its mandate on the basis of unselfish and humanistic  principles; to study carefully the allocation of its resources, particularly in the education sector; to review its
606   attitude in its selection of and support for NGOs, and to work more closely with these groups so that the desire  and will of the Director-General of UNESCO take shape in concrete deeds and do not remain mere rhetoric.
40.11 WCOTP, as in the past, states here in the present that the future can indeed be better for the children  of the world. Our partnership with UNESCO has borne fruit in the past; we look forward to even greater co-  operation with the Organization in the future, so that we may gather a great harvest in the days ahead. The  pressure of time on your busy agenda does not permit me to present a long list of suggested areas for our closer  co-operation. I will close with one which is much needed: UNICEF, in collaboration with UNESCO and all  other pertinent United Nations agencies, has been requested to ensure the effective implementation of gender-  sensitive monitoring and evaluation mechanisms to assess progress made in the reduction of disparities between  boys and girls in health care, nutrition and primary education programmes. WCOTP stands as a willing partner  in such an important collaborative undertaking, the objective of which is to ensure that all peoples will enjoy  the opportunities to develop to the limits of their potential.
40.12 Mr President, in conclusion, allow me to thank you for the opportunity of addressing this distinguished  body; while congratulating UNESCO on this important anniversary, 1 offer continued and increased co-  operation from the teachers of the world. Thank you.
(41) The PRESIDENT (Translation from the Spanish):  I thank the representative of the World Confederation of Organizations of the Teaching Profession. I  should like to remind speakers that we still have several names on our list and that they should try to observe the  five-minute time-limit. I now give the floor to Ms Freud, representative of the International Humanist and Ethical  Union.
42.1 Ms FREUD (International Humanist and Ethical Union):  Mr President, Mr Director-General, honourable delegates, ladies and gentlemen, on behalf of the  International Humanist and Ethical Union, a heartfelt good evening to you. Though time is limited, allow me  first to briefly clarify that for my association, humanism is a democratic, non-theistic, non-partisan ethical life  stance, which affirms that human beings have the right and the responsibility to give meaning and shape to  their own lives. It therefore rejects arbitrary and supernatural views of reality.
42.2 — As you can see, Mr President, our philosophers have led us right back and down to earth. Down to  earth, yes, but with visions. Visions deeply rooted, however, in Mother Earth and her surrounding skies, and  which stem from our sense of individual accountability and even more so, from our sense of responsibility  towards forthcoming generations. At this point, I like to recall that when the co-founder of my association,  Sir Julian Huxley, became the first Director-General of UNESCO, one of his main concerns was to ensure a  healthy system of checks and balances within this international community. Strongly aware of the potentially  overwhelming power and influence of Member States officials, he made sure that other levels of representation  and participation would be honoured and thus paid particular attention to the status of non-governmental  organizations.
42.3 So, right from the beginning, a guarantee was to be maintained that, in addition to their consultative  status or their right to voice ideas, concerns and hopes of a more universal nature, the non-governmental  organizations would increasingly become an integral part of UNESCO's multiple practical endeavours, projects  and, of course, visions for a better future.
42.4 Consequently, I am very happy, Mr President, to be given today the opportunity to report on a few very  worthwhile and recent ventures before proceeding, despite our awareness of continuing worldwide poverty and  man-made tragedy, to share with you our very much renewed hopes, in this stunning new era, for a better  sharing and a more humane life for all.
42.5 Our long standing active participation in the UNESCO/NGO working group for human rights and  peace, and our special emphasis on non-violent strategies and alternatives, continue to branch out into several  rewarding directions, especially in the field of education. Thus, the first international seminar on human rights
15   and the military that my association successfully organized with the support of UNESCO is proceeding with  follow-up seminars and case-studies presented by different countries on human rights and international  humanitarian law training activities for their military personnel.
42.6 Our contribution of up-to-date data and the promotion of better gun control has been recognized as an  important input for a recommendation by the working group on the status of women, by another group on the  family, for its impact on domestic violence, and by the small group of NGOs highly dedicated to alleviating the  unredeemable conditions of children passively or, worse, actively involved in armed conflicts.
42.7 At a time when we are witnessing so many uprisings, cultural and ethic reawakenings, liberations or  new dictatorships, it is more imperative than ever for UNESCO to support the promotion of non-violent conflict  resolution, lest we allow powerless despair to resort to the same means as the oppressors or fall prey to ancient  warrior cults. In this respect, we have forwarded extensive lists of peace and human rights educational material  and made known the millennium-old Iroquois ‘Great Law of Peace’, its inspiration to Benjamin Franklin, its  direct impact on the American Constitution and, by a ripple effect, though unknowingly on many progressive  trends of thought and on many leaders around the world. We urge you to study this document as it enabled the  once warring six Iroquois nations to live in peace with one another for almost a millennium and presents us  with valuable fundaments for nation-to-nation negotiation, for compromise and, above all, for decision by  consensus within a community.
42.8 Furthermore, on the eve of the proposed 1492-1992 commemorations, it is time we acknowledge the
500-year history of peace pipe smoking by the indigenous peoples of the Americas, and the quiet unrelenting  determination and courage they are still showing in their pursuit of an education based on a tradition of mutual  respect and acceptance, peace and harmony with Mother Earth.
42.9 Therefore, if we wish our international community to become increasingly all-embracing and whole,  we need flexibility and room to be made so that the aborigenous peoples who are the first nations and the  ancient guardians of the land, can take their rightful place at our side.
42.10 Aware as we all are of the worldwide abuse and rape of the environment, we need more than ever to  hear and listen to those wise philosophers of the land and acquire their nurturing knowledge, while the beat of  their drums that echoes the beat of the heart every human child has heard in the womb of its mother that  beckons us to remember that, alongside all forms of life on this planet, we are born and nurtured by Mother  Earth. When she suffers, we suffer; when she is being destroyed, we are being destroyed.
42.11 Mr President, this flight of thought may appear too simplistic in the face of all the turmoil and  frightful number of wanton fires burning right now in the world, yet it is because we are confident that under  the apparent chaos of change lies not only the ferment of life itself but also the powerful yearning for a braver  new world, that we cannot think of a better and more solid stepping-stone than our common planet to lead us  forth towards a better conviviality. Therefore, aware of ‘the compelling need to define and proclaim a new  global ethics for humankind’, the International Humanist and Ethical Union has contributed, as a modest share,  the booklet entitled 'A Declaration of Interdependence: A New Global Ethics’, which emphasizes a global moral  consensus, human rights and human responsibilities.
42.12 Indeed, now that everywhere our fellow humans are reclaiming the ultimate freedom, the freedom of  choice and consequently their potential ability to assume responsibility, it is essential that we support individual  rights, then the recognition of diversity and finally the necessity of fair sharing.
42.13 To conclude, Mr President, let us remember that should we not yet succeed in our universal  endeavours, and even, despite our efforts and goodwill, fail and fail again, that no viable solution has ever come  from inertia or the complicity of silence or from joining ranks with the knights of deceit, of hatred or of  destruction, but from the ultimate hope that arises from the human conscience that prevails and from the  knowledge that, for the sake of truth, we can always stand up, be counted and testify. Thank you, Mr President.
(43) The PRESIDENT (Translation from the Spanish):  I thank the representative of the International Humanist and Ethical Union and now call upon Ms Joan  Banks, representative of the International Soroptimist Association.
44.1 Ms BANKS (International Soroptimist Association):  Mr President, Mr Director-General, ladies and gentlemen, Soroptimist International subscribes most  strongly to the priority assigned in the Draft Programme and Budget for 1992-1993 to women.
44.2 Soroptimist International welcomes the proposed budget for the Co-ordinating Unit for Activities  relating to the Status of Women. In a negative growth situation it is a little higher than in the previous  biennium. The fact that new concrete initiatives are proposed and are considered to have high priority is most  heartening.
44.3 Soroptimist International welcomes also the increased co-operation with the United Nations  Commission on the Status of Women and relevant non-governmental organizations. We believe that the  proposed programmes, aimed at improving women's self-image and self-confidence show that emphasis has  been given to 'women in development, social change and the protection of the environment’, to quote the  Executive Board at its 136th session.
444 This is very satisfactory because the concern for women's issues is deep and genuine. However, it also  shows that inequalities, disparities and discrepancies in the different fields of competence of UNESCO, and in  all parts of the world, still prevail, for both ‘cotton women' and ‘silk women’. Soroptimist International  welcomes Mobilizing Project 1, Combating illiteracy. Recognizing that the illiteracy rate for women and girls  remains very high, and indeed is increasing, Soroptimist International gives top priority to supporting literacy  programmes in the programme area of education and supports government programmes that increase resources  for education, facilitate the training of women and encourage the achievement of higher educational and  professional qualifications by women.
44.5 Soroptimist International welcomes UNESCO's support in promoting access by girls and women to  technical and vocational education. We work to encourage education and vocational training for women in all  fields of work, including non-traditional occupations, with particular emphasis on scientific, technical and  managerial subjects. Soroptimist International is also concerned about women in rural areas. For example, in  Turkey Soroptimists have sponsored literacy courses, in co-operation with the public education directorate, to  improve the access to schooling of girls in rural and remote areas.
44.6 Soroptimist International welcomes the support for research on violence against girls and women in  cities. We have in our programme area ‘human rights and the status of women’ a priority focus on women and  violence, particularly support for victims, counselling of the family group and working to change negative  attitudes and combat degrading images of women.
44.7 The forward-looking strategies for the advancement of women lays on governments the responsibility  for preventing and eliminating violence against women. Soroptimist International supports this call to action to  secure the safety of girls and women from acts of violence and to help them overcome the consequences when  violence does occur.
448 We are interested in the cross-cultural comparative study of the problems of migrant women in four  countries of the Asia and the Pacific region. Soroptimist International has many clubs in that region and has a  priority focus in the programme area ‘economic and social development’ on the social and economic integration  of migrant women.
449 Soroptimist International is pleased to see support for studies and research on traditional practices. It  has for many years shown concern about traditional practices that disadvantage women, particularly, but not  exclusively, in the developing world, and agrees that financial assistance for research and educational  programmes should be encouraged to develop health systems directed towards the eradication of these  practices.
44.10 The recognition of a project financed by Soroptimist International in the Highlands of Cajamarca,  Peru, and a joint venture with UNICEF and grass-roots organizations in the framework of the World Decade  for Cultural Development, proves our mutual commitment.
44.11 Mr President, we look forward to our continuing co-operation with UNESCO for the benefit of a world  where, to quote the Director-General, ‘compassion fatigue' cannot be allowed to take place. I thank you.
(45) The PRESIDENT (Translation from the Spanish):  I thank the representative of the International Soroptimist Association and call upon Ms Pelzer,  representative of the International Confederation of Free Trade Unions.
46.1 Ms PELTZER (International Confederation of Free Trade Unions):  Mr President, Mr Director-General, distinguished delegates, ladies and gentlemen, we are living at a  turning-point in history which raises high expectations for a new world order. UNESCO, by its mission, is  directly challenged and faces a unique task. Since the last General Conference, the dramatic developments in  Central and Eastern Europe, the reverberating consequences of the Gulf War for the Middle East, the winds of  change in Africa, the growing momentum of international economic integration in Western Europe, the  Americas and Asia and the Pacific, and the reactivation of the United Nations as a focal point for conflict  resolution, and international co-operation in respect of the environment, mark the beginning of a new era.
46.2 For the free trade union movement, democracy, development and human rights, including workers’  and trade union rights, have always been interdependent issues - one cannot be attained without the others.  There is now reason to hope that progress will after all be possible. However, real democracy is hard daily  work, it demands constant vigilance as well as tolerance. Civil rights, as codified internationally, are essential.  Democracy without freedom of association or freedom of expression, for instance, is a sham. Over and above  guaranteed universal suffrage, citizens and their own freely chosen institutions have to be able to participate in  decision-making and exert influence over the execution of the decisions. Trade unions are a major factor in  shaping democracy; their free existence is a touch-stone for the existence of fully fledged democracy.
46.3 The ICFTU 15th World Congress, in March of next year, will be looking at these issues, at what type  of democratic society its members want - there are one hundred million of them at this point in time in all five  continents. They most surely want a democratic society capable of turning back the tide of poverty to achieve  full employment and balanced, sustainable and environmentally stable development; a democratic society  capable of preserving peace and of winning for the working people freedom to control their own lives and  determine their own futures. They will define their role in this process by working out an action programme.
464 We see a parallel way of thinking in Director-General Mayor's formulation when he underlined the  unique role of UNESCO in reconciling humankind with its environment, development with solidarity and co-  operation, and market forces with ethics. UNESCO can count on the full support and co-operation of the free  trade unions, including the International Federation of Free Teachers’ Unions and the International Federation  of Journalists, in fulfilling this role - if indeed UNESCO and its Member States let us play our role, which  cannot consist, of course, in the occasional statement. Trade unions have to be recognized as representative  organizations of workers in industry and public service, in teaching and training professions and in  communications.
46.5 We made our contributions to the elaboration of the third Medium-Term Plan two years ago, and we  are today by and large in agreement with the priorities as set out in the Draft Programme and Budget for 1992-
1993, particularly as regards basic education for all and the education and training of young people and adults  to help them participate in the changing world of today. We believe that it is important for UNESCO to help  develop concepts to equip young people and adults with the capacity for continuing education, for analytical  judgement to face changes in employment and structural adjustments as well as with the capacity to fully play  their role as emancipated citizens. ICFTU also agrees with the particular focus on girls and women. Adult  education in the process of transition towards democratic societies and towards the creation of a social market  economy is of major importance, just as it proves to be for the developing nations. The introduction of a social  infrastructure in the process of restructurization of the economy needs to be based on the participation of all  factions of the society in political life and public administration. Not least important is technical and vocational  training in this process.
46.6 In both areas we would wish to see the trade unions involved, at the level of UNESCO itself and at the  ministerial and local authority level in the Member States. We are disappointed that the World Declaration on
610   Education for All, which was adopted by the World Conference in Jomtien last year, calls for ‘strengthening  partnership’ but does not take into consideration that if partnerships - for instance between governments and  non-governmental organizations - are to be meaningful, freedom of association must be guaranteed. Trade  unions, in this context, are not mentioned at all in the Declaration. Even more with regard to technical and  vocational training and education projects, we believe it essential that not only employers be consulted but also,  of course, workers’ and teachers’ unions. As this field of education is a shared responsibility between UNESCO  and ILO the tripartite concept of the latter should be fully accepted. Without that approach the intentions may  be flawed.
46.7 The need for co-ordination of schools and training systems and the development of anticipatory  policies for economic and social development and for the personal advancement of the individual places  UNESCO in a unique position to guide the discussion within the United Nations system on the role of  education as a whole at this particular turning-point in history. It is only to be hoped that the scarce resources  which are available will not be shifted from one component of the projects to another, thus weakening the  concept. We are aware of the difficult task, but now, more than ever, it is necessary that all governments in  North, South, East and West allocate the means that are necessary to provide people and indeed society with the  educational tools needed to forge and practise democracy on the basis of freedom and equality. I thank you.
(47) The PRESIDENT (Translation from the Spanish}:  I thank the representative of the International Confederation of Free Trade Unions. The representative of  the Co-ordinating Committee for International Voluntary Service, Mr Chelikiani, now has the floor.
48.1 Mr CHELIKIANI (Co-ordinating Committee for International Voluntary Service):  Mr President, Mr Director-General, Your Excellencies, distinguished delegates, ladies and gentlemen,  I have the honour to take the floor before you, on behalf of our organization which has been inspired, housed  and supported by UNESCO since 1948. The Co-ordinating Committee for International Voluntary Service  consists of 115 member organizations, of which 13 are themselves international. We have a strong membership  in the developing countries, actively participating and sharing the decision-making process within our  Committee. Our principal mission is to harness additional energies by mobilizing the young and the less young  in the service of the community, and thereby to mould the social dimension of the individual, who makes these  efforts voluntarily.
48.2 Enjoying category A status which confers on us the privilege of consultation and association, we have  been constantly and consistently contributing to the best of our abilities to the fundamental mission of  UNESCO, which consists in elevating the quality of the human being at the spiritual, intellecinal and  biospheric levels. By promoting harmonious and judicious growth and utilization of education, science and  culture, UNESCO wishes to lay the foundations for a peaceful, prosperous and humane society. Since these  principles are enunciated, how far have we come in realizing those objectives? This question sounds  particularly harsh in the context of our current difficult situation in the Organization. My answer, though not  fully positive, is emphatically optimistic. UNESCO has not so far provided a solution but it certainly symbolizes  our common quest for universal values in the midst of diversity that sustains humanity.
48.3 Mr President, with your permission I shall make some comments accompanied by some suggestions  which are, necessarily, very brief so as not to tax the precious time of this assembly. Having brought them first  to your attention, subsequently we wish to place them before the Executive Board and the Director-General.
48.4 Firstly, in order to hold high the eminently ethical mission of UNESCO, all those associated with this  Organization as legislators, administrators and partners must feel bound by a 'moral contract’, as proposed by  the Director-General in his introduction to the general policy debate on the second day of this Conference. It  means that each one of us should set higher standards of behaviour ‘in the unrestricted pursuit of objective  truth’, as the UNESCO Constitution puts it, and demonstrate scrupulous respect for the rules of procedure,  innovative management and productivity, austerity in the use of public funds, valuing job-satisfaction more  than remuneration received from the Organization, compassion towards the less privileged and, above all, a  sense of accomplishing a historical role. These exemplary qualities will have a ‘multiplier effect’, a word that is  often repeated in this House.
48.5 Further, such a vision and such a mission on the part of the Organization should permeate all its  activities and serve as a reference point for all other United Nations agencies to be what, Mr Pérez de Cuéllar,  the Secretary-General of the United Nations yesterday called the brains of the United Nations system. A  scrutinizing mechanism within the Organization could avoid duplication of activities with other agencies.
48.6 Secondly, it is only natural that a universal vision should be reflected in the international composition  of the Organization. Here, we deplore the continued absence of some States in spite of vigorous efforts by the  Director-General. But we think that this is, above all, the direct concern of the political organ of the  Organization, i.e. the General Conference composed of the Member States. Therefore, we appeal to you, Your  Excellencies, to exert your considerable influence on those States, sharing your conviction with them that in a  democratic structure changes are induced from within. In any case, many of our NGOs and professional bodies  have members from those States and they actively participate in UNESCO activities.
48.7 Thirdly, we believe that any substantial advances in the field of democracy, human rights and freedom  are possible only in a dynamic and open society. While legitimate governments can provide a safe framework  for such a society, it is the peoples initiative, manifested through associations and movements, which can  provide the necessary stimulation and creativity. By their very nature, they are schools for democratic attitudes  and practices. It is, therefore directly within the purview of UNESCO's objectives to promote the spirit of the  voluntary sector everywhere and to support and strengthen suitable NGO structures.
48.8 It is highly desirable that UNESCO should publish every six years a report on the state of the voluntary  sector in the world, along with its sexennial report on NGOs' contribution to UNESCO's activities.
48.9 Fourthly, this Conference has repeatedly expressed its political will to achieve rapid decentralization,  but translation of such a decision into action has been regrettably slow. An external evaluation or an in-depth  study by the Executive Board would be very instructive here.
48.10 Fifthly, it is our conviction that not only the existing financial strain but also the order of things to  come justify the Organization's seeking independent sources of income, in addition to the statutory and  voluntary contributions from Member States and funds-in-trust, etc. For our part, we are convinced of the  existence of not yet fully explored possibilities to raise supplementary funds, while at the same time  communicating with and involving a wider public across the world. Income in cash and kind could be obtained  through links with national and multinational trade and industrial firms in the fields of leisure, travel, culture  and electronic and audio-visual productions. Obtaining donations of copyright, patents and royalties, sale of  universal postage stamps and issuing ‘friends of UNESCO' cards are only some examples justifying our request  for the Conference to mandate the Executive Board to make a study on this. With this, Mr President, I should  like with your permission to conclude. 1 thank you.
(49) The PRESIDENT (Translation from the Spanish):  I thank the representative of the Co-ordinating Committee for International Voluntary Service and regret  having had to interrupt him. There are still six speakers on our list and if each of them is going to speak for ten or  more minutes, there is a danger that we shall find ourselves alone with no one to listen to us, for the room is  emptying. I now call upon Mr Neville, President of Pax Romana.
50.1 Mr NEVILLE (Pax Romana):  Mr President, Mr Director-General, ladies and gentlemen, Senor Carlos Torner, President of the  student movement of Pax Romana, the International Movement of Catholic Students, addressed the twenty-fifth  session of the General Conference of UNESCO on behalf of Pax Romana. I come here today as president of the  graduate movement, the International Catholic Movement for Intellectual and Cultural Affairs. I thank you,  Mr President, for this opportunity.
50.2  Ishould like also to take this opportunity to thank UNESCO itself and particularly its Director-General  for the greatly improved co-operation between the Secretariat and non-governmental organizations both during  this biennium and at the present Conference.
50.3 It is encouraging to us to see that your Organization realizes that its purposes are very well served by  the non-governmental organizations, by their concrete actions in the field, often at the grass-roots level, or by  the creation of numerous and varied networks of international co-operation, thus creating the mutual  understanding that can transcend the barriers of cultural, racial and (regrettably) religious differences which  have so often divided the human family.
50.4 Our own organization, Pax Romana, in both its graduate and student movements, is, like UNESCO  itself, working for development which is respectful of the deepest human values - ‘cultural development’, to use  the phrase of the World Decade.
50.5 It is in relation to the World Decade for Cultural Development that, I regret, Mr President, Pax  Romana feels the need to express some concerns. While the United Nations itself, through a decision of its  General Assembly in 1986, proclaimed this Decade and jointly sponsored it with UNESCO, there is now a  feeling that the United Nations is not really willing to take the cultural dimension into account in its  development programmes. This point was made by the Director-General in his opening address to the meeting  of the Intergovernmental Committee of the World Decade for Cultural Development in February this year. He  expressed his disappointment that the recognition has not been reflected in the International Str f th  Fourth United Nations Development Decade adopted last year in New York.
50.6 This concern that United Nations development programmes ignore the cultural dimension is shared by  many non-governmental organizations, by quite a few governmental representatives and, if I may be so bold as  to say so, also by some UNESCO officials. There is perhaps a misunderstanding as to the real issue raised by  the expression ‘cultural development’. Some may think that it merely means the preservation of traditional ways  of living or forms of local architecture. While these have a value, in our view the cultural dimension in  development programmes goes much deeper. Human lives, human cultures are in a constant process of  evolution and change. Much of the change is brought about by technological advances which are often at the  heart of development programmes. The important thing 1s to involve people with the changes that development  programmes mean to them, to make them partners in these changes and to help them integrate this new  technological world with their own outlook on life.
50.7 Partnership should be a key word in the concept of development. Here non-governmental  organizations and private organizations and groups have an important role to play. This is, I think particularly  important in the huge Third World cities, where vast slums are growing, inhabited by millions of dispossessed  and marginalized people from the countryside who have come to the cities in search of a better life. In the  course of my work with Pax Romana I have visited the slums where some of our members are working in  Mexico City, Rio de Janeiro, Lima, Bombay and Calcutta. Mr President, it is easy to forget that in the Third  World there are many cities that have more people than there are in many European countries.
50.8 For an increasing proportion of mankind - the inhabitants of huge cities with up to 20 million people -  the issue is no longer the preservation of cultures which have been nurtured over generations in stable rural  environments but to develop and transmit to the unemployed and marginalized young people cultural values  which are respectful of human dignity and based on human solidarity. It is against this background,  Mr President, that we must express our deep regret that in the programme and budget for the coming biennium  the mobilizing project included in the third Medium-Term Plan on "The future of cities in the face of social and  cultural challenges’ seems to have been forgotten.
50.9 We recognize that in Programme V.2, Social change, there 1s a proposed programme action  (paragraph 05204) including consultations to develop three pilot projects towards bettering the conditions of  life of marginalized urban groups, particularly the young. In view of the need, the investment of some $486,000  seems very, very small, particularly when almost a quarter of it will be subventions for the professional  development of architects and urban planners. AH that we can say is that we hope that, although small, this  sum represents at least a beginning so that the third mobilizing project can really come into effect in the next  biennium.
50.10 In conclusion, Mr President, I would like to thank UNESCO once again for the work that it is doing in  the areas of human rights and international understanding and peace, and assure it that Pax Romana stands  ready to co-operate. Thank you, Mr President.
(51) The PRESIDENT (Translation from the Spanish}:  I thank the President of Pax Romana. I now give the floor to Mr Abdoulaye, Head of the Scientific and  Technological Research Division of the West African Economic Community.
52.1 M. ABDOULAYE (Communauté économique de l'Afrique de l'Ouest) :  Monsieur le Président, Monsieur le Président du Conseil exécutif, Monsieur le Directeur général de  l'UNESCO, Messieurs les chefs de délégation, honorables délégués des organisations internationales, je prie le  Président de la Conférence générale d'accepter mes félicitations pour sa brillante élection, ainsi que les autres  membres du bureau, et pour la manière sobre et efficace dont il a mené les débats jusqu'ici. J'associe à ces  félicitations S. Exc. M. Anwar Ibrahim pour la façon magistrale dont il a dirigé la séance d'ouverture et surtout  pour la teneur de son allocution, dont j'approuve sans réserve les idées qui y sont développées.
52.2 — Avant de m'adresser à M. le Directeur général, permettez-moi de parler de ce qui nous réunit : la  Conférence générale. La discussion sur le programme et budget revient aux Etats. Permettez-moi seulement de  dire qu'à la CEAO, nous avons constaté la maîtrise technique qui a caractérisé l'élaboration du budget,  remarquable par sa flexibilité qui lui permet de s'adapter immédiatement aux changements qui viendraient à  intervenir dans les programmes.
52.3 — L'UNESCO est notre affaire à tous et notre souci est de la voir retrouver son universalité et sa  mondialité, selon le terme cher à Mme Gisèle Halimi. Les efforts du Directeur général de l'UNESCO que je suis  depuis quelques années seront certainement couronnés de succès et notre conviction est faite à la CEAO que si  les démembrements de l'ONU devaient disparaître, l'UNESCO au moins devrait rester.
52.4 Je voulais au départ limiter mon intervention aux relations UNESCO/CEAO mais la morosité du  monde qui déteint sur les travaux de la présente session m'obligent à parler politique. Depuis cinq ans, le  monde connaît de grands bouleversements qui ont atteint leur apogée cette année en URSS. Certains on dit ici  que c'est le triomphe de la liberté. Mais il entraîne avec lui une nouvelle donne politique où les repères  n'existent plus. Aucune clarté dans tout ceci, en dehors du bouquet de soleil qui nous vient des pays baltes, que  je salue ici. Le tiers monde se sent délaissé au profit de l'Est. Voilà qu'on lic l'aide à l'application de la  démocratie dans nos pays. En effet, en dehors de la famille des Nations Unies et des ONG, la démocratie nous  est donnée avec parcimonie. La démocratie ? Parlons-en. "Existe-t-elle vraiment ?" En dehors des Etats-Unis et  de l'Inde, je ne vois pas de vraies démocraties dans le monde. Mon continent est déboussolé depuis deux ans par  ce nouveau jeu de quilles. Nous avons été colonisés. Les peuples d'Afrique et en particulier d'Afrique de l'Ouest  ont payé de leur sang la libération du pays colonisateur pendant les deux dernières guerres mondiales. Mais,  consciemment ou non, nous avons intégré cette colonisation qui fait partie de notre histoire. On ne nous a  appris pendant ce temps que l'anti-démocratie (bourrage des urnes en particulier pour faire passer le candidat  de la métropole). Nous avons une démocratie à l'africaine faite de "modus operandi” et de "modus vivendi”  fondés sur nos traditions. Si on doit introduire une démocratie à l'occidentale, ce doit être à doses  homéopathiques. Les soubresauts que l'on constate ne concernent pas l'Afrique profonde. Ils s'arrêtent aux  microcosmes des villes et l'on verra vite que les changements qui sont intervenus cà et là ne sont pas porteurs  de solutions, sauf au Cap-Vert peut-être.
52.5 Il faut maintenant faire la part des choses. L'Afrique est riche mais sa richesse n'a servi qu'à  développer les pays industrialisés. D'ailleurs, elle a commencé à les développer depuis la traite des nègres. Au  moment des indépendances, on n'empruntait pas. On avait accès aux droits de tirage spéciaux. Mal conseillés,  nous avons privilégié le secteur primaire au détriment d'une industrialisation ratée. En un mot, nous avons  mangé notre pain blanc. Mais la solution n'est pas de se confondre en invectives et en frictions qui ne font que  creuser davantage le fossé qui se creuse entre le Nord et ie Sud. Que l'on ne s'y trompe pas surtout. Tous les  pays, à de rares exceptions près, sont en crise et on ne peut pas forcer la main à celui qui ne veut pas vous  donner de l'aide.
52.6 La solution de nos problèmes passe par notre prise en charge par nous-mêmes. Cela suppose avant tout  une volonté politique de vouloir se développer. Elle passe de façon obligée par la maîtrise de la technologie. J'y  reviendrai si vous le voulez bien dans mon adresse au Directeur général de l'UNESCO. C'est de cette dernière  que nous avons besoin pour cela, car elle est seule à gérer le Thésaurus SPINES. Mais elle a besoin pour cela de  quiétude et d'une certaine marge de manoeuvre. Il s'agira notamment de nous aider de façon incitative et
614   normative et de la charger un peu moins de résolutions pour accroître sa marge de manoeuvre dans son  Programme de participation qui nous intéresse à la CEAO. Mais ce sont les hommes qui font les programmes et  il faudrait pour cela plus d'harmonie, plus de paix dans la maison. C'est un "melting pot” de races et ce devrait  Être déjà un laboratoire de civilisation universelle. C'est pourquoi les problèmes de personnel doivent trouver  des solutions harmonieuses et faire cesser les antagonismes.
52.7 Monsieur le Directeur général, les relations privilégiées entre l'UNESCO et la CEAO existent depuis
1978. C'est l'UNESCO qui a lançé les projets CRES (Centre régional d'énergie solaire) et EMIG (Ecole des  mines et de géologie), basés respectivement à Bamako et à Niamey. Par la suite, il y a eu l'établissement du  protocole régissant la recherche scientifique et technique à la CEAO. Mais il y a eu surtout le projet  RAF/78/067 tripartite UNESCO/PNUD/CEAO, qui avait pour but de créer les conditions favorables à  l'implantation de la science et de la technologie dans notre sous-région. Ce projet a eu des résultats appréciables  que la conjoncture défavorable à partir de 1984 n'a pas permis d'exploiter. Il s'agit entres autres de la  détermination des priorités scientifiques de la CEAO ; de l'inventaire du potentiel scientifique et technique des  pays de la CEAO, qui a été fait en 1983 ; de l'organisation de la politique scientifique à la CEAO ; de  l'adoption d'une méthodologie appropriée de la coopération interinstitutionnelle ; du projet de création du  FOCOST (Fonds communautaire pour la science et la technique) ; dans le cadre de l'innovation, du projet de  création de l'AGECOVAR (Agence communautaire de valorisation des produits de la recherche). Ces projets  sont repris en grande partie avec d’autres dans les requêtes faites dans le cadre du cinquième cycle du PNUD. Si  significatifs que soient les résultats du projet RAF/78/067, la vérité commande de dire qu'il a manqué un peu de  pédagogie. En effet, le second conseiller qui devait être engagé pour la partic informatique du projet ne l'a pas  été et les crédits de son poste ont été alloués à des activités.
52.8 — C'est forts de tous les enseignements qu'il nous a laissés que nous émettons le voeu, Monsieur le  Directeur général, qu'un nouveau projet RAF/92 soit élaboré pour gérer les projets qui figurent dans notre  programme soumis au PNUD dans le cadre du cinquième cycle de programmation. Il s'agit pour l'essentiel de  projets scientifiques et technologiques, de biotechnologie et de questions qui nous tiennent particulièrement à  coeur en ce qu'ils touchent à l'innovation technologique : la liaison recherche-industrie et formation-emploi  ainsi que la valorisation de la recherche scientifique et technique pour la préservation de l'environnement.
52.9 Monsieur le Directeur général, nous avons beaucoup investi dans les ressources humaines. Nous avons  cinq établissements d'enseignement supérieur et de recherche. Ils sont surdimensionnés pour notre sous-région  et grèvent cruellement le budget de la communauté qui les fait fonctionner et nous obèrent par le poids de la  dette. Vous pourriez mener la réflexion avec nous pour en faire des centres d'excellence au niveau de la région,  et pourquoi pas, du continent, et surtout pour faire en sorte qu'ils assurent eux-mêmes leur propre  fonctionnement.
(53) The PRESIDENT (Translation from the Spanish):  I give you 15 seconds to conclude your statement.
54, M. ABDOULAYE (Communauté économique de l'Afrique de l'Ouest) :  Monsieur le Directeur général nous avons maintenant un credo à la CEAO. Le mal dont souffre notre  continent est certainement économique et financier mais notre véritable handicap, c'est le fossé technologique  qui le sépare des pays développés et nous a transformés en véritables consommateurs passifs de technologie.  Faute d'acquérir dès maintenant la maîtrise et le réflexe technologiques, cette situation perdurera encore des  siècles, Il n'est que de voir le Japon qui autrefois était un pays médiéval et qui à force de pratiquer la méthode  de la redécouverte a acquis la maîtrise qui lui a permis d'utiliser celle de la découverte. Aujourd'hui le Japon est  la principale puissance technologique du monde. L'adage veut que nul ne soit prophète en son pays. C'est  pourquoi vous pouvez nous aider, Monsieur le Directeur général, à sensibiliser nos décideurs à l'importance de  la science et la technologie.
(55) The PRESIDENT (Translation from the Spanish):  I regret this more than you, Mr Abdoulaye. First of all we passed you a note; we then gave you  additional time. The other speakers are waiting. You could have shortened your statement.
56.1 M. ABDOULAYE (Communauté économique de l'Afrique de l'Ouest) :  Les trois domaines d'intervention de l'UNESCO ont une interaction, mais nous souhaiterions que dans  notre intérêt, tout concourent à la maîtrise de la technologie, qui est non seulement une discipline d'éveil mais  une méthodologie.
56.2 On parle beaucoup d'éducation, de la culture un peu moins, un peu moins encore de la science et très  pudiquement de la technologie. Un orateur a souligné ici les dangers potentiels que récèle la technologie. Mais  pour nous il s'agit de plonger dedans. Sans cela, comme l'a dit le professeur Ki Zerbo, "nous aurons toujours les  pieds dans le néolithique et la tête dans la stratosphère”. La maîtrise de la technologie est un passage obligé  pour le développement de l'Afrique. Il ne faut pas s'attendre à recevoir des cadeaux par le transfert de  technologie. Il faut s'y mettre tout de suite et imiter le Japon. Une fois la technologie maîtrisée, les pauvres  d'aujourd'hui seront les riches de demain.
56.3 Monsieur le Directeur général, depuis la nomination du professeur Lissouba à la direction du ROSTA  de Nairobi, celui-ci a connu un essor pour le moins extraordinaire et couvre tout le continent, L'assistance que  le ROSTA donne à la CEAO a dépassé les normes habituelles pour se placer au niveau de l'assistance  privilégiée. C'est pourquoi je ne puis passer sous silence son rôle et vous prie de lui faire part de la plus grande  reconnaissance de notre Organisation, à lui et tout son personnel, surtout au moment de son départ définitif,  que nous avons appris.
